# FWD-017 governing stack full-read transcript (current after ledger/map update)

===== BEGIN AGENTS.md =====
# AGENTS.md — Worker Operating Harness

## Role
You are a repo worker executing the project harness, not a conversational assistant producing isolated turns. Your job is truthful, lawful, durable progress through the active repo control surface.

## Goal
Advance the live project state by selecting the next lawful leaf, reading required authority/files, executing bounded work, proving it, recording evidence, and continuing until a real stop condition is reached.

## Control-surface freeze
`AGENTS.md` and `PLANS.md` are frozen standing control surfaces once accepted. Routine execution must not mutate either file for progress tracking, gate bookkeeping, status refreshes, historical cleanup, or ordinary continuation hardening. Use `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` for mutable live execution state.

A future mutation to `AGENTS.md` or `PLANS.md` is lawful only when:
- the user explicitly opens that exact control surface; or
- a concrete governance defect cannot be truthfully resolved in `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`, a receipt, or a report alone.

Any such mutation is one bounded governance-control leaf and must preserve `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` as the mutable live pointer.

## Success criteria
A worker pass is successful only when it:
- reads live authority before mutation;
- selects one lawful leaf at a time;
- executes, proves, records, and persists evidence;
- performs bounded continuation search;
- continues to the next lawful leaf when lawful work remains.

A checkpoint, commit, proof pass, package, receipt, ledger update, or clean handoff state is a continuation point, not a stop point.

## Harness loop
Repeat:
1. inspect Git/repo state;
2. read live authority;
3. select the next lawful leaf;
4. read required files in full;
5. execute lawful bounded work;
6. run relevant proof;
7. record receipts, manifests, diffs, and ledger state where required;
8. perform bounded continuation search;
9. continue.

A response boundary is not an execution boundary. The user does not need routine progress updates.

## Stop rules
Stop only when one of these exact conditions is true:
1. observable context failure prevents coherent continuation;
2. hard runtime/tool failure prevents continuation after lawful recovery attempts;
3. explicit user stop, handoff, or output request exists;
4. explicit live authority forbids further execution;
5. bounded continuation search proves no lawful executable work remains.

Do not stop for checkpoint creation, package creation, proof pass, commit, receipt, ledger update, gate completion, tranche completion, branch creation, queue exhaustion, handoff preparation, a tidy response opportunity, or temporary inability to identify the next leaf.

## Continuation search
Failure to immediately identify the next executable leaf is not a stop condition. Before concluding that no lawful executable work remains, inspect at minimum:
- current `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` state;
- active receipts and manifests;
- unresolved blockers;
- unresolved validation gaps;
- stale evidence risks;
- proof coverage gaps;
- authority inconsistencies;
- queue integrity state;
- manifest integrity state;
- current-pointer continuity;
- admissible audit, control, evidence, manifest, receipt, ledger, or planning-hardening leaves within the active authority class.

If a lawful executable leaf exists, select it, record the selection where required, and continue.

## Blocker rule
A blocker starts bounded resolution. It is not a stop condition.

When a selected leaf encounters a blocker:
1. read the nearest authority and affected files;
2. search the repo for relevant symbols, paths, configs, receipts, and tests;
3. determine the smallest lawful local resolution;
4. apply it only if the active authority permits it;
5. run targeted validation;
6. continue.

If unresolved, record it truthfully, mark affected claims `Unknown` or `blocked`, preserve evidence, and continue another lawful leaf where possible.

## Evidence and truthfulness
Do not claim `read`, `reviewed`, `verified`, `fixed`, `implemented`, `passed`, `complete`, `aligned`, `reconciled`, or `audited` unless direct evidence supports the claim.

Material claims require at least one of: paths, line ranges, command output, receipts, manifests, diffs, patches, artefacts, or official documentation. Otherwise label claims `Inferred`, `Unknown`, or `Unverified`.

Evidence must be reproducible, attributable, bounded, and inspectable.

## Read-before-mutate rule
Before substantive mutation, fully read:
- `AGENTS.md`;
- `PLANS.md`;
- `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`;
- applicable route overrides;
- governing normative documents;
- governing execution plans or ledgers;
- all directly impacted files for the active leaf.

Do not replace required full reading with search, grep, AST parsing, embeddings, summaries, metadata, inventories, line counts, headings, filename inference, or partial sampling.

If full reading is infeasible, do not fabricate comprehension. Bound the unread material truthfully, avoid unlawful mutation based on unread material, and continue other lawful work where possible.

## FULL_READ_MANIFEST rule
Before substantive mutation, create or update the current `FULL_READ_MANIFEST` required by the active control surface.

For each required file, record path, reason for inclusion, read status, and mutation relevance.

Do not rely on stale manifests as current proof. Validate the explicit current-target manifest before closure claims. Manifest completion alone does not authorise stopping.

## Authority hierarchy
Authority order:
1. direct user instruction;
2. `AGENTS.md`;
3. `PLANS.md`;
4. `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`;
5. governing normative documents;
6. route override files;
7. tests;
8. implementation code;
9. comments and docstrings.

Lower authority may not override higher authority. If authorities conflict, attempt lawful bounded resolution first. If unresolved, record the conflict precisely and continue other lawful work where possible.

## Governance order
Runtime behaviour must follow this order:
1. normative intent;
2. tests;
3. implementation;
4. documentation.

Tests govern runtime behaviour, but passing tests alone are not proof of correctness. Do not implement behaviour before governing intent and governing tests exist or are lawfully updated. False-positive resistance and evidence quality matter.

## Atlas governance
Atlas material is candidate intent only. Atlas content is non-authoritative until lawfully admitted.

Required migration order:
1. normative intent;
2. tests;
3. implementation;
4. evidence;
5. ledger update.

Do not directly mutate runtime behaviour from Atlas material outside lawful migration flow.

## Boundary discipline
Work one bounded leaf at a time. Do not silently broaden scope, batch unrelated authority classes, skip evidence recording, skip verification, or pivot authority surfaces without recording it.

Do not mutate runtime/source/tests/config/vocabulary/Atlas/generated slices unless live authority explicitly opens that boundary.

`_archive/*.tar.gz` remains excluded/skip-worktree unless explicitly routed into scope by live authority.

## Git discipline
Before mutation, confirm repository and branch state. Use isolated worker branches where practical.

Do not rewrite unrelated files, discard unrelated changes, or destroy audit history.

After mutation, preserve diffs or patches before closeout when feasible.

## Proof cadence
Before checkpoint, handoff, or closure claims, run the proof commands required by live authority.

At minimum, when available and relevant, run:
- `git status --short`;
- `git diff --check`;
- targeted tests for changed behaviour;
- current manifest validators;
- current registry or surface-record validators;
- active proof-block checks.

If validation cannot be run, state exactly why and run the next best local check.

## Packaging rule
Packaging preserves state; it does not authorise stopping. Creating tarballs, zips, checkpoints, bundles, manifests, receipts, commits, or proof logs does not end execution. After packaging, continue lawful work unless a stop rule is met.

## Closeout rule
A final response is allowed only when a stop rule is met.

If stopping because continuation became impossible, state the precise blocker, what was inspected, what changed, proof status, the next lawful gate or leaf, and where evidence is preserved.

Use `Known true`, `Ruled out`, and `Next gate` when useful for state compression.

## Response style
Be concise, evidence-backed, mechanically precise, and non-theatrical. Avoid fake certainty, aspirational completion language, ceremonial narration, and vague summaries where exact state is knowable.

The worker is not rewarded for stopping safely at checkpoints. The worker is rewarded for truthful continuity, bounded lawful progress, auditability, and persistence under uncertainty.

===== END AGENTS.md =====

## 1. Objective

Create a primary active-code Atlas after surface-specific full-read adjudication: a repo-native evidence corpus that maps current active source, tests, configs, data, contracts, schemas, receipts, and docstrings into the same eighteen architecture surfaces used by the Atlas Bridge.

The active-code Atlas must be shaped to compare mechanically against:

1. the recovered Atlas intent corpus in `var/audit/imports/atlas_bridge_inputs/legacy_intent_atlas.json`;
2. the Atlas Bridge/native satisfaction ledger in `var/audit/ledgers/intent_migration/native_intent_satisfaction_ledger.json`;
3. the eighteen Atlas surface slices under `var/audit/atlas_surface_slices/slices/`;
4. the current active repo reality under `src/`, `tests/`, `config/`, `data/`, `docs/`, and `var/audit/`.

This plan does not create or claim the completed active-code Atlas. It controls the work required to create it.

## 3. Preserved repo hierarchy

The project hierarchy remains:

```text
normative documents → tests → code → code docstrings
```

For this workstream that means:

1. The active-code Atlas records what the current repo appears to do; it does not make current code authoritative.
2. A current-code behaviour is not accepted as correct unless it is supported by normative authority and proof.
3. Atlas donor intent and Atlas Bridge mappings remain evidence only.
4. Tests must be written or updated before later behaviour refactors or feature imports.
5. Docstrings are lowest authority and must be recorded separately from runtime behaviour.

## 5. Active-code Atlas target shape

The active-code Atlas must use a parallel shape so future comparison can join active reality against donor intent and Bridge routing without translation theatre.

Future output target:

```text
var/audit/active_code_atlas_001/active_code_atlas.json
```

Target top-level shape:

```json
{
  "ledger_name": "active_code_atlas",
  "schema_version": "1.0-draft",
  "status": "structural_inventory_scaffold_static_only_or_surface_adjudicated",
  "source_repo_head": "<git commit>",
  "source_package_sha256": "<if applicable>",
  "records": []
}
```

Target active-code record fields:

| Active-code Atlas field | Mirrors / relates to | Required meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `active_code_record_id` | `atlas_record_id` | Stable ID for one active-code behaviour or contract unit. |
| `active_behaviour_unit_id` | `intent_unit_id` | Grouping key for related active-code records. |
| `source_file` | `source_file` | Active repo path, not donor path. |
| `source_line_start` | `source_line_start` | First active-code evidence line. |
| `source_line_end` | `source_line_end` | Last active-code evidence line. |
| `record_type` | `record_type` | Active record class, for example `runtime_behaviour`, `schema_contract`, `test_contract`, `config_contract`, `receipt_surface`, `docstring_claim`. |
| `construct_type` | `construct_type` | Module/class/function/dataclass/pydantic model/test/fixture/config/data/receipt/docstring/etc. |
| `symbol_name` | `symbol_name` | Import path, test node ID, config key, data identifier, or receipt identifier. |
| `evidence_status` | `evidence_status` | `static_structural_observation`, `observed_by_full_read_adjudication`, `observed_by_test`, `docstring_only`, `inferred`, `unresolved`, or `blocked`. |
| `observed_behaviour` | `observed_behaviour` | What the active repo evidence actually shows. |
| `intended_behaviour` | `intended_behaviour` | Normative expectation when derivable from governing docs/tests; otherwise `unknown`. |
| `inputs` | `inputs` | Observed active inputs/carriers/fixtures/config keys. |
| `outputs` | `outputs` | Observed active outputs/carriers/receipts/state changes. |
| `state_reads` | `state_reads` | Active state/config/data/repository reads. |
| `state_writes` | `state_writes` | Active writes to state, receipts, files, stores, ledgers, or repos. |
| `external_dependencies` | `external_dependencies` | Imports, package dependencies, files, clocks, network/API/broker surfaces, environment variables. |
| `packet_or_schema_effects` | `packet_or_schema_effects` | Packet/schema/model/carrier consequences. |
| `config_or_coefficient_effects` | `config_or_coefficient_effects` | Config/coefficient/modifier consequences. |
| `thresholds_or_literals` | `thresholds_or_literals` | Hard-coded values, thresholds, enums, constants, defaults. |
| `branch_conditions` | `branch_conditions` | Decision branches and guarded paths. |
| `error_null_empty_semantics` | `error_null_empty_semantics` | Error, null, blank, empty, malformed, and missing-input behaviour. |
| `side_effects` | `side_effects` | Non-return effects. |
| `known_bug_or_defect_caveat` | `known_bug_or_defect_caveat` | Evidence-backed caveat, or null. |
| `intent_unit_summary` | `intent_unit_summary` | Human-readable summary of the active behaviour group. |
| `operating_model_role` | `operating_model_role` | Role in the deterministic desk-cognition operating model. |
| `blueprint_surface` | Bridge `blueprint_surface` | One of the eighteen architecture surfaces, or `blocked_blueprint`. |
| `blueprint_surface_status` | Bridge `blueprint_surface_status` | Surface implementation-truth status from blueprint/current map. |
| `target_owner` | Bridge `target_owner` | Native owner from blueprint/current map. |
| `lawful_owner_candidate` | `lawful_owner_candidate` | Candidate active owner path or module. |
| `owner_confidence` | `owner_confidence` | `direct`, `inferred`, `unresolved`, or `conflict`. |
| `proof_family` | `proof_family` | Tier/test/proof family relationship, not a pass claim. |
| `active_test_evidence` | Bridge `existing_test_decision` / proof context | Tests/proof blocks that touch the behaviour; may be empty. |
| `docstring_evidence` | Bridge `docstring_declaration_required` | Docstring claim paths/lines, kept separate from runtime proof. |
| `native_satisfaction_status` | Bridge `native_satisfaction_status` | `satisfied`, `partially_satisfied`, `missing`, `transform_required`, `blocked`, or `unknown`. |
| `status` | `status` | Active-code Atlas record state: `observed`, `partial`, `blocked`, `unresolved`, or `superseded`. |
| `blockers` | `blockers` | Missing reads, authority blockers, proof blockers, or ambiguity. |
| `exclusion_reason` | `exclusion_reason` | Why a candidate was excluded from the active-code Atlas, or null. |
| `notes` | `notes` | Evidence notes only; no unsupported interpretation. |

The field names deliberately keep the Atlas vocabulary where the semantics are equivalent. Active-code-specific fields are added only where needed to preserve Bridge comparison and proof/docstring separation.

## 6. Scope

In scope for `ACTIVE_CODE_ATLAS_001`:

- Create the active-code Atlas method and schema.
- Build the active-code Atlas one architecture surface at a time.
- Use the eighteen blueprint surfaces as the primary segmentation.
- Read selected active source/test/config/data/docstring evidence before writing records for that surface.
- Preserve every active-code record with file paths and line ranges.
- Record proof posture without claiming proof passed unless command output exists.
- Produce validation scripts or schema checks only after the schema is frozen by an earlier gate.
- Update receipts and `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` after every pass.

Out of scope unless explicitly opened:

- Runtime trading behaviour changes.
- Source implementation changes.
- Test implementation changes.
- Dependency changes.
- VENV creation or repair.
- `black` or `ruff` execution for the planning setup gate.
- Original Atlas/Bridge JSON mutation.
- Generated Atlas slice mutation or rebuild.
- Vocabulary mutation or alias admission.
- Treating active code output as normative truth.
- Refactoring code from the Atlas, Bridge, or active-code Atlas.

---

## 7. Required reading rule

Before relying on a file, read it with enough care to avoid missing important details. For controlling authority and files materially governing the selected gate, full reading is required unless infeasible.

Search, grep, headings, manifests, JSON inspection, AST parsing, line counts, summaries, embeddings, schema extraction, or generated ledgers may assist discovery. They do not replace careful reading of important in-scope files.

For active-code Atlas content gates, each selected source/test/config/data/docstring file that contributes records is in scope and must be read before records are written from it. If a file is not read, the affected record must say `unread_material` in `blockers` and must not claim direct observation.

Do not claim a file was read, checked, reviewed, verified, fixed, implemented, passed, complete, or aligned unless the work was actually done and evidence is available.

---

## 7A. Mandatory FULL_READ_MANIFEST control

Before any surface record, test extension, source repair, receipt, proof-closure statement, or ledger update, the worker must write a `FULL_READ_MANIFEST` for the controlling files and selected material files.

Each manifest entry must include:

- `path`;
- `byte_count`;
- `line_count`;
- `sha256`;
- `read_method`;
- `full_read=true` or `full_read=false`;
- `chunk_ranges` when reading was chunked;
- `notes` only where needed.

Search, grep, AST extraction, generated inventories, summaries, and prior chat memory may assist discovery but cannot satisfy the full-read requirement. If full sequential reading cannot be proven for a selected leaf, the worker must record that leaf as blocked with exact unread/infeasible files and mark affected claims `Unknown`; during a user-authorised continue-until-tokens run, route to the next lawful control/audit/evidence/manifest/receipt/ledger hardening leaf rather than treating the single blocked leaf as a user-facing stop condition.

If a file is later used as evidence but is absent from `FULL_READ_MANIFEST`, the claim must be labelled `unread` or `inferred` and cannot support mutation, proof closure, or ledger closure. If a file changes after the manifest entry was created, the manifest entry is stale and must be regenerated before that file can be relied on.

Historical `FULL_READ_MANIFEST` files are point-in-time receipts only. They may prove what a prior worker claimed against that prior checkout state, but they must not be cited as current proof after the listed files change. Only a current, validator-passing manifest for the active target may support the active target's closure claim. The active target manifest must be regenerated after any mutation affecting a listed file. The manifest validator no-argument mode checks every retained manifest under `var/audit/active_code_atlas_001/full_read_manifests/`, including historical point-in-time manifests; use explicit current target manifest paths for current-pass closure proof unless the selected leaf is auditing the historical manifest universe itself. Do not include the validator's mutable side-effect output file `var/audit/active_code_atlas_001/reports/validate_full_read_manifest_output.json` in the same manifest being validated, because the validator rewrites that file during validation and would make the manifest stale by construction; treat it as side-effect proof output, not material input evidence for that manifest.

Workers are procedurally blocked from saying `read`, `checked`, `verified`, `complete`, or `closed` for a file-backed claim unless an auditable current-or-historical manifest entry supports the exact checkout state and files used for that claim.

The generated all-surface structural inventory remains navigation/scaffold only. It cannot satisfy or bypass a `FULL_READ_MANIFEST`.

---

## 8. Git and checkpoint rules

For an execution workspace:

1. Confirm active repo root.
2. Confirm branch and `git status --short`.
3. If `.git` is absent, initialise Git and commit the extracted baseline before making changes.
4. Do not include `.venv`, caches, bytecode, temporary extraction folders, or large transient archives in commits.
5. Commit after a coherent proof-backed planning/control pass when useful for durable state.
6. A commit is a checkpoint, not proof that the active-code Atlas exists.
7. Before packaging or external handoff, record branch name, commit hash, status, changed files, validation command output, artefact paths, and hashes.

---

## 8A. Execution cadence and no ordinary gate pauses

Passing a gate, proof block, targeted test, validation command, commit, package, or ledger update is a continuation point, not a user-facing stop point. A worker must continue to the next lawful target while current user authority and the live ledger still authorise the work.

Do not pause at ordinary gate boundaries merely to ask whether to proceed. During a user-authorised long run whose current instruction says to continue until tokens are exhausted, continue through the surface-by-surface workflow until context/token exhaustion, tool/runtime exhaustion, or an explicit user stop/report/handoff/package instruction prevents further lawful work in the current response.

Durability checkpoints and package smoke tests preserve work; they do not create an automatic recursive obligation to index/package the immediately previous package again. After recording useful checkpoint proof, continue to the next distinct lawful control/audit/evidence/manifest/receipt/ledger/planning-hardening leaf unless the user explicitly asks for recursive checkpoint indexing.

Diagnostic hygiene scans are discovery aids, not proof substitutes. To avoid recursive self-amplification, default live-control scans should target `AGENTS.md`, `PLANS.md`, `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`, current receipts/reports/manifests, and selected small artefacts; exclude prior scan-output artefacts, including raw scan text, scan-classification reports/receipts, prior proof-output transcripts, large generated JSON, stdout captures, and historical pre-repair logs unless the selected leaf explicitly audits them. A prior proof-output transcript may record an expected point-in-time failure and must not be treated as live proof status when a later current manifest/report records validator-passing supersession. A prior scan-classification artefact may quote historical hits and must not be treated as a fresh live-control hit unless the selected leaf explicitly audits that artefact. Record any exclusion pattern in the scan report.

The following are bounded-leaf blockers, not user-facing stop conditions while other lawful control/audit work remains available:

1. a required controlling artefact or selected in-scope file is missing or unreadable;
2. active authorities contradict each other and the conflict cannot be resolved by the authority order in this plan;
3. proof or validation fails and the lawful repair path for that leaf is unclear or would cross a closed authority boundary;
4. the next attempted step would mutate a closed surface, including runtime trading behaviour, financial/risk authority, dependency files, original Atlas/Bridge JSON, generated Atlas slices, vocabulary data/admissions, or unapproved refactor work;
5. a bounded leaf or queue subset has been exhausted.

For each bounded-leaf blocker, record the blocker in the relevant receipt/ledger, mark affected claims `Unknown` or `blocked`, and select the next lawful control, audit, evidence, manifest, receipt, ledger, or planning-hardening leaf inside current authority. Gate reports remain required inside receipts, `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`, commits, and final handoff evidence. They are not permission pauses.


## 9. Gate sequence

### Gate 0 — Planning control-surface setup

Purpose: install this plan, seed the active-code Atlas schema draft from the observed Atlas/Bridge shape, and update the mutable ledger.

Pass criterion:

- root `PLANS.md` points to `ACTIVE_CODE_ATLAS_001`;
- root `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` points to `ACTIVE_CODE_ATLAS_001_GATE_1_AUTHORITY_AND_SCHEMA_LOCK` or an equivalent next gate;
- an active-code Atlas record-shape draft exists under `var/audit/active_code_atlas_001/`;
- no source, runtime, dependency, test implementation, vocabulary, original Atlas, or original Bridge mutation occurs;
- patch/commit/package evidence is produced.

Fail criterion:

- active authority forbids the planning-doc change;
- Atlas/Bridge shape is ignored or replaced by an unrelated catalogue;
- required authority files are missing;
- root governance is accidentally deleted rather than superseded.

Allowed changes:

- `PLANS.md`;
- `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`;
- planning receipt(s) under `var/audit/active_code_atlas_001/`.

### Gate 1 — Authority and schema lock

Purpose: freeze the active-code Atlas schema before content extraction.

Pass criterion:

- controlling authority files are read for the gate;
- Atlas intent shape and Bridge shape are recorded as schema inputs;
- eighteen architecture surfaces are copied from governing blueprint/current map evidence, not from memory;
- schema validation rules are written for required fields, enum fields, source path/line fields, and Bridge join fields;
- no active-code content records are generated yet except schema examples explicitly marked as examples.

Fail criterion:

- schema cannot preserve Atlas/Bridge comparability;
- schema permits records without evidence path and line range;
- schema collapses tests, code, and docstrings into one ambiguous truth field.

Allowed changes:

- active-code Atlas schema files;
- schema-lock receipt;
- ledger update.

### Gate 2 — Surface inventory and file selection

Purpose: select the active files to read for one architecture surface.

Pass criterion:

- exactly one architecture surface is selected unless user explicitly authorises a multi-surface pass;
- selected files are listed by path and reason;
- selected tests/proof blocks are listed separately from source/config/data/docstrings;
- unread files are not used for direct-observation records.

Fail criterion:

- files are selected only by name similarity without blueprint/current-map evidence;
- source and test truth are mixed;
- a runtime-sensitive or reserved surface is opened beyond read-only evidence extraction.

Allowed changes:

- surface inventory report;
- ledger and receipt updates.

No source or test changes.

### Gate 3 — Active evidence reading and record extraction

Purpose: create active-code Atlas records for the selected surface from read evidence.

Pass criterion:

- every record has source path and line range;
- runtime behaviour, tests, configs, receipts, data, schemas, and docstrings are recorded as separate evidence classes when they differ;
- docstring-only matches are labelled `docstring_only`;
- name matches alone are rejected;
- current-code output is not treated as normative truth.

Fail criterion:

- a record lacks evidence;
- a record asserts correctness without normative/test proof;
- a record imports Atlas donor language as active behaviour without active-code evidence.

Allowed changes:

- active-code Atlas records for the selected surface;
- extraction receipt;
- ledger update.

No source or test changes.

### Gate 4 — Active-code Atlas validation

Purpose: validate the active-code Atlas records for schema, evidence, joinability, and internal consistency.

Pass criterion:

- schema validation passes;
- required source path and line fields are present;
- record IDs are unique;
- architecture surface values match the governed eighteen-surface vocabulary or explicit `blocked_blueprint`;
- proof/docstring/code evidence classes remain separable.

Fail criterion:

- duplicate IDs exist;
- records cite missing paths without blockers;
- records claim implemented behaviour without code and proof evidence;
- records cannot join to Bridge/Atlas comparison fields.

Allowed changes:

- validation report;
- repair to active-code Atlas records only where evidence supports the repair;
- ledger and receipt updates.

### Gate 5 — Three-way comparison plan

Purpose: prepare the later comparison between Atlas intent, Atlas Bridge, and the active-code Atlas.

Pass criterion:

- comparison keys are defined;
- statuses are defined for `already_satisfied`, `partially_satisfied`, `missing`, `transform_required`, `blocked`, and `conflict`;
- future Tier A/B/C test work is queued from normative gaps, not current-code output;
- implementation/refactor remains closed.

Fail criterion:

- comparison treats donor Atlas as runtime authority;
- comparison treats active code as correctness oracle;
- test work is skipped before refactor.

Allowed changes:

- comparison plan/report;
- ledger and receipt updates.

No source or test implementation changes.

---

## 10. Validation expectations

For Gate 0 planning setup, validation is limited to:

```bash
git status --short
git diff --check
git diff -- PLANS.md ROADMAP_LEDGER.md var/audit/active_code_atlas_001/
```

Do not create a VENV for Gate 0.
Do not run `black` or `ruff` for Gate 0.
Do not run the full test suite for Gate 0.

Later content gates must define their own narrow validation commands before execution.

## 12. Surprises and discoveries

- The uploaded package contains 1,716 Atlas/Bridge records, not 1,719.
- The Atlas intent corpus has two object-order keysets but the same logical field set.
- The Bridge/slice machinery already uses eighteen architecture surfaces and 1,716 joined records.
- A primary active-code Atlas has not yet been accepted; the existing all-surface artefact is a structural inventory scaffold only.

## 15. Official-source escalation rule

If a selected leaf is blocked by uncertainty about a platform, SDK, API, cloud service, dependency, CI behaviour, test-framework behaviour, or vendor-specific behaviour, do not choose a direction until authoritative documentation has been checked. During a user-authorised continue-until-tokens run, record the selected leaf as blocked, use only official sources for the relevant vendor or platform, including AWS, Google, Microsoft, IBM, OpenAI, and project-specific upstream documentation, and either resolve the uncertainty inside the leaf or route to the next lawful control/audit/evidence/manifest/receipt/ledger hardening leaf. Do not rely on forums, blogs, Stack Overflow, Reddit, or anecdotes unless the user explicitly authorises non-authoritative sources. Record the citation or source path in the decision log.

## 16. Required closeout evidence for real stops or explicit reports

Do not use this section as permission to stop. A closeout/report is required only when `AGENTS.md` / Stop rules and Closeout rule permit a real stop, or when the current user instruction explicitly asks for a report, stop, handoff, package, or closeout.

When a real closeout/report is required, report:

- exact real stop condition, explicit user-requested report boundary, or context/tool/token exhaustion condition;
- what changed;
- what was read;
- what was not read but relevant;
- changed files;
- validation commands and exit statuses;
- receipts/ledger paths;
- commit hash or patch path if created;
- package path/hash if created;
- current `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` pointer;
- Known true / Ruled out / Next gate.

Do not claim the primary active-code Atlas exists until surface-specific full-read content gates confirm or rewrite records from read evidence and validation evidence. The structural inventory scaffold is not enough.

# ROADMAP_LEDGER.md

Mutable execution ledger for NVDA10 active-code Atlas planning and execution. Historical Atlas roadmap and test false-positive resistance audit state is retained below as evidence.

Status: **live mutable ledger**
Owner: active execution thread / PM thread / Codex worker applying a bounded tranche
Location when installed: repository root as `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`
Last seeded from: `var/audit/ledgers/atlas_tranche_roadmap_ledger.json` and current post-vocabulary workflow state
Do not freeze this file. Update it every pass.

---

## 1. Purpose

This file is the live execution pointer for the current active-code Atlas workstream, while retaining the prior Atlas roadmap and test false-positive resistance audit history as evidence.

`PLANS.md` defines the standing workflow. This ledger defines **current state**.

The model must not infer current phase from chat memory, old bridge briefs, or stale Gate 2/Gate 3 planning language. It must read this file and follow the live pointer below.

---

## 2. Operating rule

1. Read `AGENTS.md`.
2. Read `PLANS.md`.
3. Read this file: `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`.
4. Use `Current pointer` below as the next work target unless evidence shows it is stale.
5. Execute the target through the project hierarchy:

   ```text
   normative documents → tests → code → docstrings → proof → receipts/ledger
   ```

6. Update this ledger before any package, handoff, commit, or stop.
7. Continue to the next lawful row while authorised scope remains.

Passing gates, proof, commits, packages, or target selection are continuation points, not stop points.

### Historical five-hour execution runway note — ROADMAP_SUPPORT_SPINE_5H_BRANCH_001

This subsection is retained historical support-spine planning evidence only. It is not live authority to reopen docs, tests, source, proof-block manifests, Makefile checks, retained-test registry entries, or support-spine rows unless the current pointer table or explicit current user instruction selects that boundary.

The historical support-spine set was:

1. `5.2 Raw capture and source temporal law` — historical then-next target after external source/API ingress row 012 — preserve source temporal standard truth at raw bundle boundaries without opening external APIs or dependencies.
2. `5.3 Raw signal families` — trace-only upstream signal support where needed for non-interference and coverage proof.
3. `5.5 Prepared ingress and runtime context preparation` — deterministic prepared substrate and checkpoint handoff surfaces.
4. `5.6 DMPV3 packet carriage` — canonical packet shells for prepared context and review lineage surfaces.
5. `5.12 Review and explanation layer` — deterministic explanation lineage and missing-material flags.

Current execution must obey the live current pointer and hard boundaries below: runtime trading behaviour, financial/capital/risk decision policy, dependency files, external network/API clients, original Atlas/Bridge JSON, generated Atlas slices, historical receipts, runtime/source/test mutation, vocabulary mutation, and unrelated refactor work remain closed unless explicitly opened by current live authority.

The user-directed continuation law in `AGENTS.md` controls long-running execution: work continues until context/token exhaustion unless the current user instruction explicitly states a different stopping condition. A passing leaf, proof, commit, package, row update, or exhausted bounded leaf is a continuation point, not a stop condition while lawful control/audit work remains available.

---

## 2A. Dependency-aware surface order for active-code Atlas execution

The blueprint row numbers are taxonomy labels, not execution order. Current worker passes must follow this dependency order unless live authority or new evidence forces a recorded change:

1. `5.18 Vocabulary governance interface` — accepted bounded pass; do not restart without concrete defect.
2. `5.17 Testing, proof, and checkpoint surfaces` — accepted/repaired bounded proof infrastructure; do not restart without concrete defect.

### 4.5 Five-hour run sizing rule for future execution

A future long run is under-sized if it performs only one neat repair and stops after packaging. A properly sized run must contain at least one of:

- a multi-row planning pack covering all held rows;
- a multi-leaf implementation branch with several test families and proof blocks;
- a reconciliation branch that compares commits, reset-safe packages, receipts, and ledger state;
- a full authority-brief pack plus one selected bounded implementation branch if the user explicitly opens that authority.

Packaging is a durability checkpoint. It is not a stopping reason unless the user has explicitly requested a pause, as in this planning pass.

## 6. Execution-state vocabulary

Use these exact state values in the roadmap table.

| State | Meaning | Selection rule |
|---|---|---|
| `acted_on_bounded` | The row/tranche has received bounded docs/tests/code/proof work, but the whole row is not claimed complete. | Do not reselect unless a new sub-tranche is explicitly opened. |
| `next_selected` | The row/tranche is the current pointer. | Execute next. |
| `in_progress` | Work is underway and must be reconciled before selecting another row. | Continue or reconcile. |
| `pending_lawful` | Candidate remains available when higher-priority lawful work is done. | Select when pointer advances. |
| `pending_support` | Supporting governance/ledger/proof work likely needed soon. | May run before runtime-sensitive rows. |
| `hold_runtime_authority` | Runtime/trading/financial-sensitive. | Do not select without explicit user authority. |
| `hold_reserved` | Reserved architecture row. | Do not select unless row authority changes. |
| `blocked` | Known blocker. | Do not select until blocker is resolved and ledger is updated. |
| `unknown` | Not enough current evidence. | Inspect before selection. |

---

## 8. Update rule after every pass

Before stopping, packaging, or handing off, update this ledger.

Minimum update fields:

| Field | Required update |
|---|---|
| Current pointer | Move it if the current tranche was acted on, blocked, or superseded. |
| Roadmap table row | Update `Live execution state`, `Current rule / next action`, and `Latest evidence / receipt`. |
| History table | Add one row for the pass. |
| Proof | Record command/result/receipt path in history or row evidence. |
| Boundary | Record any explicit hold or newly opened authority. |

A pass may not leave `Current pointer` aimed at a tranche that is already `acted_on_bounded`, `blocked`, `hold_runtime_authority`, or `hold_reserved`.

---

## 9. Next-selection rule

If `Current pointer` is stale, missing, or points at a row that should not be selected:

1. Start from the roadmap execution table above.
2. Exclude rows with states:
   - `acted_on_bounded`
   - `hold_runtime_authority`
   - `hold_reserved`
   - `blocked`
3. Prefer the highest-ranked remaining `pending_support` or governance-support lane before runtime-sensitive lanes.
4. Prefer rows that strengthen proof, ledgers, testability, source temporal law, or deterministic review before trading behaviour.
5. Record the new pointer in this file before implementing.

---

## 12. Hard boundaries

Unless explicitly opened by the user, keep closed:

| Boundary | Rule |
|---|---|
| Runtime trading behaviour | Closed. Do not alter trading actions, position sizing, live decisions, or order path behaviour. |
| Source implementation changes | Closed unless the current pointer or explicit current user instruction opens the exact source boundary. Test-first ordering alone does not open source mutation. |
| Test implementation changes | Closed unless the current pointer or explicit current user instruction opens the exact test boundary. |
| Financial/risk authority | Closed unless explicitly opened. |
| Dependency changes | Closed unless explicitly authorised. |
| Original Atlas/Bridge JSON | Read-only evidence. Do not mutate. |
| Generated Atlas slices | Read-only evidence unless a rebuild is explicitly authorised. |
| Bridge/candidate queue generation | Do not rebuild during normal execution. |
| GitHub mutation | Only Codex/user environment pushes. Sandbox produces patches/tarballs unless explicitly authorised otherwise. |

---

## 13. Installation note

Save this file at the root of the repository as:

```text
ROADMAP_LEDGER.md
```

Then update `PLANS.md` with one small pointer:

```md
## Live roadmap ledger

The live execution state is held in `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` at repo root.
Read it after `AGENTS.md` and `PLANS.md`.
It is mutable and must be updated after every real pass.
If `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` exists, do not infer current phase from old Gate 2/Gate 3 planning text.
```

## 14. Forward programme — full 18-surface active-code reconciliation and rewrite

Status: `live_forward_programme`

Purpose: open a long-run, ledger-defined programme so future workers cannot lawfully stop after the old 074/075 bounded-search closure. This section plans the remaining active-code reconciliation and rewrite across all eighteen architecture surfaces.

Authority basis:

- `AGENTS.md` controls worker behaviour and fixed intake order.
- `PLANS.md` remains the frozen standing plan.
- This `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md` remains the mutable live pointer.
- `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` defines the eighteen architecture surfaces and their target architecture law.
- `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md` and `docs/02_BRIEFING_AND_EXECUTION_OPERATING_MODEL_CORE.md` define the governed runtime sequence and behavioural boundaries.
- `docs/08_TESTING_AND_PROMOTION_CORE.md` requires proof before promotion.
- Atlas, Bridge, active-code Atlas, receipts, manifests, and historical pass records are evidence. They are not direct runtime authority.

### 14.1 Programme-state rule

The prior state value `acted_on_bounded` means a surface has received bounded work. It does not mean the surface is complete, mature, implemented, reconciled, promoted, or closed for all future work.

The forward programme uses these exact state values:

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `planned_forward_reconciliation` | Surface is ordered for future active-code reconciliation and rewrite. |
| `opened_forward_reconciliation` | Worker has opened the surface and created current read/proof evidence. |
| `authority_read_current` | Current authority stack and surface-specific authority have been read in full for the current repo state. |
| `atlas_bridge_compared_current` | Current active code, active-code Atlas evidence, Atlas intent, and Bridge evidence have been compared for the surface. |
| `tests_first_defined` | Required test work has been defined before source mutation. |
| `tests_written_or_repaired` | Tests have been added or repaired before source mutation. |
| `source_reconciled` | Source/config/fixture changes have been made inside the surface boundary. |
| `proof_passed_current` | Targeted and required broader proof have passed in the current repo state. |
| `bounded_closed_current` | Surface row has current evidence, receipts, ledger status, and proof sufficient to move to the next row. |
| `blocked_current` | Surface row is blocked by evidence with the blocker recorded and the next lawful continuation row selected. |

### 14.2 Long-run continuation rule

A worker pass must continue through ordered rows while lawful work remains.

The following are continuation points, not stop conditions:

- finishing `FWD-000`;
- finishing one internal gate inside a surface;
- finishing one surface;
- adding a test;
- fixing a source file;
- passing a targeted test;
- passing a proof block;
- updating a manifest;
- updating a receipt;
- creating a commit;
- creating a package.

A worker stops only when the active pointer has been advanced as far as lawful evidence permits in the current repo state, a frozen authority mutation would be required, a required file or external service is absent with no local substitute, safe Git/filesystem/validation work is impossible after local recovery, or the user explicitly stops the run.

### 14.3 Required internal gates for every surface row

Every surface row from `FWD-001` to `FWD-018` uses this internal gate sequence.

| Gate | Name | Required result |
|---|---|---|
| `.0` | Surface open | Declare surface ID, architecture row, exact allowed mutation class, and prior bounded history. |
| `.1` | Authority full read | Read root controls, full numbered normative stack, docs/11 surface block, route overrides, test override if tests are touched, and surface-specific receipts/manifests. |
| `.2` | Evidence inventory | List active code, tests, config, fixtures, receipts, active-code Atlas evidence, Atlas intent slice, Bridge records, and current map rows for the surface. |
| `.3` | Three-way comparison | Compare intended behaviour, Bridge target owner, and active repo evidence. Mark satisfied, partial, missing, transform-required, conflict, reserved, or blocked. |
| `.4` | Test-first plan | Define required Tier A/B/C proof and false-positive resistance before source mutation. |
| `.5` | Test mutation | Add or repair tests only inside the surface boundary. |
| `.6` | Source/config mutation | Modify source/config/fixtures only after tests define the invariant. |
| `.7` | Targeted proof | Run the narrow surface tests and proof block named by the row. |
| `.8` | Broader proof | Run retained-test/proof-block validation required by the touched surfaces. |
| `.9` | Receipt and ledger close | Record read manifest, proof outputs, diff/patch, receipt path, updated ledger state, known true, ruled out, and next row. |

No `.9` gate is permission to stop. `.9` moves directly to the next ordered row.

### 14.4 Global read set for each surface

Every surface row requires this baseline read set before mutation:

1. `AGENTS.md`
2. `PLANS.md`
3. `ROADMAP_LEDGER.md`
4. `docs/00_CONTROL_SURFACE_ROUTING_INDEX.md`
5. every numbered `docs/*_CORE.md` and present `docs/*.1_*_WORKING.md` companion from 01 through 10
6. `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`
7. `NVDA10_CURRENT_STATE_ARCHITECTURE_MAP.md`
8. `tests/AGENTS.override.md` when tests are touched
9. `config/retained_test_registry.yaml` when tests or proof tiers are touched
10. `config/proof_blocks.yaml` when proof blocks are touched
11. surface-specific active-code Atlas records, Atlas intent slice, Bridge rows, receipts, manifests, source files, tests, config files, fixtures, docs, and code docstrings selected by that row

Historical manifests may guide discovery. They do not replace current full read for a row.

### 14.5 Forward execution order

This is the forward implementation/reconciliation order. It deliberately separates foundation surfaces from runtime surfaces, then follows the governed runtime direction from upstream truth to downstream action and review.

# 00_CONTROL_SURFACE_ROUTING_INDEX

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.

## Routing boundary

This document routes readers to the owning live control document. It does not define doctrine, precedence, behavioural law, process law, or execution scope.

Read this document after `AGENTS.md` and before the numbered control documents.

## Stack rule

`*_CORE.md` files hold the frozen baseline.

`*.1_*_WORKING.md` files hold admitted mutable execution-local supplementation.

Read the core file first.

Read the working companion only when it contains admitted local material for the bounded question.

## Routing table

- `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`

===== BEGIN docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md =====
# 01_NORMATIVE_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.

## Authority hierarchy

When documents conflict, precedence is:

1. this document
2. other `.md` files in `docs/`
3. repository configuration files
4. scripts, tests, and source code
5. ephemeral planning or chat material

No lower-precedence surface overrides, weakens, or silently reinterprets a rule stated here.

Dedicated control documents elaborate this core inside their own ownership boundaries. They do not override it.

## Frozen repo invariants

This repo exists to emulate the cognition flow of a Tier 1 NVDA desk trader at Goldman Sachs, including the human judgement lens behind that desk behaviour.

The runtime is deterministic. It is not an autonomous LLM trader.

Repo truth is governed by documents, contracts, and controlled proof.

The system must remain replayable, auditable, and explainable.

Done is binary. Done means correct, unambiguous, internally consistent, and fully reconciled where required.

A work item that is partially correct, ambiguous, internally inconsistent, or unreconciled where reconciliation is required is not done.

Normative doctrine governs tests and code. Tests and code conform to doctrine.

## Governed runtime sequence

1. real-data admission and runtime preparation
2. financial-calendar reference and projection
3. temporal context
4. market regime context
5. options and flow context
6. posture and risk permission
7. playbook eligibility
8. expression and execution
9. downstream pre-deployment gate
10. capital state, bounded sizing, and capital deployment authority
11. downstream opening architecture
12. execution bridge, execution receipt, and downstream state feedback
13. live inventory, position state, and close architecture
14. review and explanation surfaces

A governed step must not be collapsed, merged, skipped, reordered, or reclassified without explicit normative change.

## Serial desk-cognition spine

The serial desk-cognition spine consists of:

1. temporal context
2. market regime context
3. options and flow context
4. posture and risk permission
5. playbook eligibility
6. expression and execution

The serial desk-cognition spine forms the candidate.

The serial desk-cognition spine does not own the independent parallel risk lane.

The serial desk-cognition spine does not own capital deployment authority.

## Parallel risk lane

The independent parallel risk lane runs alongside the governed runtime sequence and remains distinct from the serial desk-cognition spine, the downstream pre-deployment gate, capital deployment authority, and downstream execution and inventory surfaces.

The independent parallel risk lane is not a numbered stage of the serial desk-cognition spine.

The independent parallel risk lane does not form the candidate.

The independent parallel risk lane does not allocate capital.

## Cross-cutting governed layers

State-conditioned modifiers and coefficient authority act across the governed runtime sequence. They do not replace governed runtime steps.

DMPV3 governs live inter-module packet carriage. If a runtime output is packetised, it uses the canonical DMPV3 outer envelope.

## Runtime model boundary

This document owns top-level repo truth, authority hierarchy, frozen repo invariants, the governed runtime sequence, and the top-level serial/parallel/capital/downstream boundaries.

Detailed operating behaviour is owned by `docs/02_BRIEFING_AND_EXECUTION_OPERATING_MODEL_CORE.md`.

Domain contracts and bounded domain meanings are owned by `docs/03_DOMAIN_MODEL_CORE.md`.

Technical architecture is owned by `docs/04_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE_CORE.md`.

Guardrails and anti-pattern law are owned by `docs/05_GUARDRAILS_CORE.md`.

# 02_BRIEFING_AND_EXECUTION_OPERATING_MODEL_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Operating model

The repo emulates the cognition flow of a Tier 1 NVDA desk trader at Goldman Sachs.

The system does not operate as an abstract prediction engine.

The system operates as disciplined desk behaviour under institutional constraints.

The binding design lens is human desk judgement under institutional discipline.

The operating model behaves as one ordered runtime sequence:

1. real-data admission and runtime preparation
2. financial-calendar reference and projection
3. temporal context
4. market regime context
5. options and flow context
6. posture and risk permission
7. playbook eligibility
8. expression and execution
9. downstream pre-deployment gate
10. capital state, bounded sizing, and capital deployment authority
11. downstream opening architecture
12. execution bridge, execution receipt, and downstream state feedback
13. live inventory, position state, and close architecture
14. review and explanation surfaces

No governed step is optional.

No governed step is skipped, merged, reordered, or reclassified without explicit normative change.

## Serial desk-cognition behaviour

The main desk-cognition spine is serial and ordered.

Its six stages are:

1. temporal context
2. market regime context
3. options and flow context
4. posture and risk permission
5. playbook eligibility
6. expression and execution

The serial desk-cognition spine forms the candidate.

Each stage has a bounded behavioural job.

A later stage does not take over the job of an earlier stage.

An earlier stage does not pre-empt the bounded job of a later stage.

## Independent parallel risk behaviour

The independent parallel risk lane runs alongside the governed runtime sequence while the serial desk-cognition spine forms the candidate.

The independent parallel risk lane produces an independent reading of the present environment.

That reading does not become candidate formation.

That reading remains distinct from the downstream pre-deployment gate and capital deployment authority.

## Downstream pre-deployment behaviour

The downstream pre-deployment gate blocks incomplete, disallowed, or unusable candidate material before capital deployment authority acts.

The downstream pre-deployment gate does not form the candidate.

The downstream pre-deployment gate does not decide fresh-capital deployment.

## Capital deployment behaviour

Capital deployment authority operates downstream of candidate formation and the downstream pre-deployment gate.

Its behavioural role is to decide whether fresh capital is deployed against the formed candidate, and in what bounded way.

It consumes preserved earlier-step truth including capital state and bounded sizing surfaces.

It does not replace serial candidate formation.

It does not replace the independent parallel risk reading.

The operating model preserves a clean behavioural distinction between:

- candidate formation
- environmental risk reading
- downstream pre-deployment blocking
- fresh-capital deployment judgement

## Downstream architecture behaviour

The downstream opening architecture preserves the authorised opening state after capital deployment authority.

The execution bridge, execution receipt, and downstream state-feedback surfaces preserve post-deployment execution truth.

The live-inventory, position-state, and close architecture preserves open-position and close-path truth after opening state exists.

These downstream surfaces do not replace candidate formation, the independent parallel risk reading, or capital deployment authority.

## Human judgement under deterministic implementation

The runtime is deterministic.

The operating model preserves human institutional judgement within that deterministic runtime.

The system preserves bounded judgement about:

- environment
- permission
- caution
- deployment restraint

The operating model is invalid if those behaviours are flattened into opaque mechanical output or replaced by unconstrained improvisation.

## Explanation requirement

Explanation is part of the operating model.

The repo must remain able to state, in plain language:

- what candidate the serial desk-cognition spine formed
- what the independent parallel risk lane concluded about the environment
- what the downstream pre-deployment gate blocked or allowed
- what capital deployment authority decided about fresh capital
- what downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close surfaces preserved

If those answers cannot be stated distinctly, the operating model is not being preserved.

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

# 03_DOMAIN_MODEL_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Main domain shape

The repo models a bounded institutional trading cognition system for NVDA.

The system has these main domain parts:

- real-data admission and runtime preparation
- financial-calendar reference and projection
- a six-stage serial desk-cognition spine
- Parallel Risk Lane
- a downstream pre-deployment gate
- capital state, bounded sizing, and capital deployment authority
- downstream opening architecture
- execution bridge, execution receipt, and downstream state feedback
- live inventory, position state, and close architecture
- review and explanation surfaces

That structure is binding unless explicitly and lawfully revised.

## Real-data admission and runtime preparation

Real-data admission and runtime preparation are a separate part of the system.

Their job is to admit source truth and prepare runtime-ready truth before serial desk-cognition begins.

Their job is not candidate formation.

Their job is not capital deployment.

## Financial-calendar reference and projection

Financial-calendar reference and projection are a separate part of the system.

Their job is to preserve calendar truth and projected calendar context for later governed steps.

Their job is not candidate formation.

Their job is not capital deployment.

## Serial desk-cognition spine

The serial desk-cognition spine is the path that moves from prepared runtime truth to candidate formation.

It has six stages:

1. temporal context
2. market regime context
3. options and flow context
4. posture and risk permission
5. playbook eligibility
6. expression and execution

These six stages are real domain boundaries.

They are not labels of convenience.

Later refinement inside a stage is lawful, but it does not erase a stage boundary without explicit lawful revision.

## Parallel Risk Lane

The Parallel Risk Lane is a separate part of the system.

Its job is to assess the present environment while the serial desk-cognition spine forms the candidate.

Its job is not candidate formation.

It remains separate from the serial desk-cognition spine even when both are working from the same market state.

## Downstream pre-deployment gate

The downstream pre-deployment gate is a separate part of the system.

Its job is to block incomplete, disallowed, or unusable candidate material before capital deployment authority acts.

Its job is not candidate formation.

Its job is not the fresh-capital decision itself.

## Capital architecture

Capital state, bounded sizing, and capital deployment authority are a separate downstream part of the system.

Their job is to decide whether fresh capital is deployed against an already formed candidate, and in what bounded way.

Their job is not candidate formation.

Their job is not general portfolio management.

Their job is not close-position logic.

They remain separate from both the serial desk-cognition spine and Parallel Risk Lane.

## Downstream architecture

The downstream opening architecture is a separate part of the system.

Its job is to preserve opening-state truth after capital deployment authority acts.

The execution bridge, execution receipt, and downstream state-feedback surfaces are a separate part of the system.

Their job is to preserve execution truth after opening state exists.

The live-inventory, position-state, and close architecture are a separate part of the system.

Their job is to preserve open-position and close-path truth after opening state exists.

These downstream parts do not replace candidate formation, the independent parallel risk reading, or capital deployment authority.

## What must stay separate

The domain model requires the system to keep these things separate:

- admitting and preparing runtime truth
- preserving financial-calendar truth
- forming the candidate
- assessing the environment in parallel
- blocking unusable candidate material before capital deployment
- deciding whether fresh capital is deployed
- preserving downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close truth
- explaining those separate results clearly

The domain model is wrong if those things collapse into one blended concept.

## Review and explanation surfaces

The system must be able to explain, in plain language:

- what runtime truth was admitted and prepared
- what financial-calendar truth was preserved
- what candidate was formed
- what the environmental assessment was
- what the downstream pre-deployment gate blocked or allowed
- what the fresh-capital decision was
- what downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close surfaces preserved

If the system cannot state those answers separately, the domain model is not being preserved.

## Vocabulary discipline

Domain terms remain stable and deliberate.

A new durable term does not appear where an established term already exists for the same meaning.

Near-synonyms that blur domain boundaries are not permitted.

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

# 04_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Architectural shape

The architecture has these top-level technical parts:

- a real-data admission and runtime-preparation path
- a financial-calendar reference and projection path
- a six-stage serial runtime path
- a separate parallel risk runtime path
- a downstream pre-deployment and capital-deployment path
- a downstream opening, execution, state-feedback, inventory, and close path
- a review and explanation path

That grouped structure is binding at the technical-lane level unless explicitly and lawfully revised.

The detailed native architecture-surface order, target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability are owned by `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` inside its bounded architecture scope.

These grouped technical lanes must be interpreted as summaries over the detailed `docs/11` architecture surfaces, not as a competing surface count.

## Normalised packet-carriage layer

The technical architecture uses DMPV3 as the normalised outer envelope for live inter-module packet carriage.

That common outer envelope applies to packetised outputs including:

- serial stage outputs
- the parallel risk runtime path output
- financial-calendar packet emitters
- imported-module packet emitters
- review carriage where runtime outputs are packetised

The architecture uses one common outer envelope and module-specific payload contracts.

A common outer envelope does not merge bounded technical responsibilities.

Packet normalisation must not collapse:

- the real-data admission and runtime-preparation path
- the financial-calendar reference and projection path
- the serial runtime path
- the parallel risk runtime path
- the downstream pre-deployment and capital-deployment path
- the downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close path
- the review and explanation path

A module does not keep an alternative outer packet grammar once DMPV3 is the live packet-carriage law.

## Cross-cutting technical layers

State-conditioned modifiers and coefficient authority act across the governed runtime sequence.

Those layers do not replace bounded runtime-path responsibilities.

## Real-data admission and runtime-preparation path

The real-data admission and runtime-preparation path admits source truth and produces prepared runtime truth.

This path is technically separate from the serial runtime path.

This path does not form the candidate.

## Financial-calendar reference and projection path

The financial-calendar reference and projection path preserves financial-calendar truth and projected calendar context.

This path is technically separate from the serial runtime path.

This path does not form the candidate.

## Serial runtime path

The serial runtime path is the main technical path through the system.

It is implemented as an ordered six-stage structure:

1. temporal context
2. market regime context
3. options and flow context
4. posture and risk permission
5. playbook eligibility
6. expression and execution

This path produces the candidate.

The technical architecture preserves that ordered structure.

Later refinement inside a stage is lawful, but it does not erase, merge, renumber, or bypass a stage boundary without explicit lawful revision.

## Parallel risk runtime path

The parallel risk runtime path is structurally separate from the serial runtime path.

Its technical role is to evaluate the present environment in parallel while the serial runtime path forms the candidate.

It does not form the candidate.

The architecture preserves that separation in runtime handling.

The parallel risk runtime path remains identifiable as its own path in implementation and review.

## Downstream pre-deployment and capital-deployment path

The downstream pre-deployment gate sits downstream of candidate formation.

Its technical role is to block incomplete, disallowed, or unusable candidate material before capital deployment authority acts.

Capital deployment authority sits downstream of the downstream pre-deployment gate.

Its technical role is to consume the formed candidate together with the required earlier-step outputs and issue a bounded fresh-capital decision.

This path does not replace the serial runtime path.

This path does not replace the parallel risk runtime path.

The architecture preserves its downstream placement and bounded technical role.

## Downstream opening, execution, state-feedback, inventory, and close path

The downstream opening architecture preserves opening-state truth after capital deployment authority acts.

The execution bridge, execution receipt, and downstream state-feedback surfaces preserve post-deployment execution truth.

The live-inventory, position-state, and close architecture preserve open-position and close-path truth after opening state exists.

These downstream parts remain structurally separate from the serial runtime path, the parallel risk runtime path, and capital deployment authority.

## Required technical separation

The architecture keeps these things separate in implementation:

- real-data admission and runtime preparation
- financial-calendar reference and projection
- candidate formation
- environmental risk evaluation
- downstream pre-deployment blocking
- fresh-capital deployment decision
- downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close handling
- review of those results

The architecture is wrong if those technical parts collapse into one blended runtime path.

## Inspectable runtime handling

The architecture keeps the main technical parts inspectable.

A reviewer must be able to determine:

- what the real-data admission and runtime-preparation path produced
- what the financial-calendar reference and projection path produced
- what the serial runtime path produced
- what the parallel risk runtime path produced
- what the downstream pre-deployment gate blocked or allowed
- what capital deployment authority decided
- what downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close surfaces preserved

The architecture is not acceptable if those results are reconstructed only by guesswork.

## Bounded technical responsibilities

Each major technical part has a bounded responsibility.

A part does not silently absorb the job of a neighbouring part because it is convenient in code.

The architecture preserves bounded technical responsibilities between:

- the real-data admission and runtime-preparation path
- the financial-calendar reference and projection path
- the serial runtime path
- the parallel risk runtime path
- the downstream pre-deployment and capital-deployment path
- the downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close path
- the review and explanation path

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

# 04.1_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE_WORKING

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.

## Local supplement

One bounded local supplement is admitted below. The core file otherwise governs in full.

## Repo-wide source temporal standard

This section admits the repo-wide source temporal standard.

This standard sits above DMPV3 packet carriage.
It governs raw captures, local capture files, derived raw surfaces, persistence records, workbook-derived specimens, replay/proof surfaces, and packets where relevant.
It is provider-agnostic.
It must support IBKR-style market data, FRED-style date-level data, and workbook-derived mixed-date reference-ledger specimens without overfitting to one provider.

The governed source temporal fields are:

- `source_time_raw`: the exact source-supplied time token as captured from the source artefact
- `source_time_kind`: the declared kind of source temporal truth carried by the source artefact
- `source_timezone_raw`: the source-supplied timezone or offset label when the source declares one
- `source_time_utc`: the UTC-normalised source observation time only when lawful source truth exists to normalise it
- `source_date`: the source observation date carried or derived without inventing finer precision than the source supplied
- `captured_at_utc`: the UTC time when the repo captured or ingested the source artefact
- `persisted_at_utc`: the UTC time when the repo persisted the record or artefact
- `source_provider`: the upstream provider identity for the observed source artefact
- `source_channel`: the acquisition channel or provider-local lane for the observed source artefact
- `time_granularity`: the source observation granularity admitted by the source truth

`source_time_utc` and `source_date` record source observation truth.
`captured_at_utc` and `persisted_at_utc` record repo process timing.
`captured_at_utc` and `persisted_at_utc` must not be substituted for source observation time.

Where the source provides only date-level truth, `source_date` is authoritative and no finer source timestamp must be invented.
Where the source provides local-clock time with a declared timezone or offset, `source_time_raw`, `source_timezone_raw`, and `source_time_utc` must remain mutually consistent.
Where a workbook-derived specimen blends fields with different source dates, the specimen must preserve field-level provenance rather than fabricate one false same-moment source timestamp.

## Rules

- Do not weaken or silently reinterpret the core companion.
- Record only bounded mutable execution-local material here.
- If this file has no admitted local material, the core file governs.

## Prepared context support-spine supplement

`ROADMAP_SUPPORT_SPINE_5H_BRANCH_001` admits a bounded support-spine handoff from raw source-temporal material into prepared runtime context, canonical packet carriage, and deterministic review explanation.

The prepared context handoff is a support surface. It may preserve source temporal standard truth, preparation lineage, derived-field names, missing optional enrichments, failed enrichments, and deterministic packet fingerprints. It must not decide trades, position size, capital deployment, risk permission, or broker instructions.

The lawful packetised support-spine output is a `prepared_context_packet` emitted by the prepared-ingress authority. Review/explanation may consume packet lineage and emit review-only explanation material, but that explanation does not become runtime decision authority.

# 05_GUARDRAILS_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Core guardrail rule

Convenience is not authority.

A change is invalid if it makes the repo easier to operate, easier to explain, or easier to code while breaking the declared structure, boundaries, or meanings defined by the live control documents.

## Structural guardrails

The following moves are prohibited unless explicitly and lawfully revised:

- collapsing real-data admission and runtime preparation into untracked side effects
- erasing the financial-calendar reference and projection step as a governed runtime step
- collapsing the six-stage serial desk-cognition spine into an unstructured path
- erasing a stage boundary inside the serial desk-cognition spine
- merging the serial desk-cognition spine and the independent parallel risk lane into one runtime path
- turning the independent parallel risk lane into part of serial candidate formation
- bypassing the downstream pre-deployment gate before capital deployment authority acts
- turning capital deployment authority into part of serial candidate formation
- turning capital deployment authority into general portfolio management
- turning capital deployment authority into close-position logic
- collapsing downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close architecture into one blended function with candidate formation or capital deployment authority
- collapsing candidate formation, environmental assessment, downstream pre-deployment blocking, and fresh-capital decision into one blended function
- allowing a compatibility surface to replace a more truthful preserved surface

## Authority guardrails

The following moves are prohibited:

- bypassing the live control stack through chat interpretation
- treating retained evidence as live authority
- using a lower-priority surface to weaken or reinterpret a higher-priority surface
- widening scope by implication
- letting a routing document act as a doctrine document
- letting a doctrine document act as a router

## Vocabulary guardrails

The following moves are prohibited:

- introducing a new durable term where an established term already exists for the same meaning
- using near-synonyms that blur a domain boundary or technical boundary
- renaming a concept in one live control surface while leaving the related live control surfaces unreconciled
- using casual wording that weakens a bounded role, boundary, or prohibition

Vocabulary is controlled to stop meaning drift.

## Explanation guardrails

The following moves are prohibited:

- presenting candidate formation, environmental assessment, downstream pre-deployment blocking, and fresh-capital decision as if they were the same result
- hiding which part of the system produced which result
- requiring guesswork to reconstruct what happened
- collapsing different outputs into one blended explanation for convenience

The repo is not acceptable if a reviewer cannot tell, in plain language, what each major part concluded.

## False-closeout guardrail

The following moves are prohibited:

- claiming work is done when known contradictions remain unreconciled
- claiming work is done when required control surfaces disagree about the current state
- claiming work is done when the stated repo truth is not the actual repo truth

A clean narrative does not make incorrect work complete.

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

===== END docs/05_GUARDRAILS_CORE.md =====
