# 06_PROCESS_AND_TRANCHE_LAW_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Scope of this document

This document owns:

- active-pack law
- execution read sequence
- one-gate-at-a-time law
- document-touch law
- state-integrity law
- receipt law
- closeout law

## Execution read sequence

Substantive execution must read in this order:

1. `AGENTS.md`
2. the active bounded pack surface in scope
3. `docs/00_CONTROL_SURFACE_ROUTING_INDEX.md`
4. `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`
5. the numbered live authority documents that own the active question, in the order required by the active bounded pack where that order does not contradict higher-priority live law
6. `PLANS.md` when the active bounded pack routes it as the repo-level plan standard
7. the active bounded pack execution-plan surfaces that define the current gate
8. retained evidence only after all live authority above has been read

Conflict precedence is owned by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

## Active-pack law

Substantive repo work must run through one active bounded pack.

The active bounded pack must be declared together by:

- repo-root `AGENTS.md`
- `docs/06.1_PROCESS_AND_TRANCHE_LAW_WORKING.md` when it contains admitted local material

For the current programme stage, the active bounded pack is the pack jointly declared by current live route surfaces:

- repo-root `AGENTS.md`
- `docs/06.1_PROCESS_AND_TRANCHE_LAW_WORKING.md` when it contains admitted local material
- `PLANS.md` when that pack routes it as the repo-level plan standard
- the ordered repo-local ExecPlan surfaces that pack routes

No file becomes part of the active bounded pack by implication.

No file becomes mutable merely because it is nearby, relevant, or historically related.

## One-gate-at-a-time law

Only one gate is active at a time.

Only one bounded objective is active inside that gate at a time.

A gate is closed only when the touched live control surfaces and required receipts agree on the same state.

## Document-touch law

Every active bounded pack must classify relevant surfaces as:

- touched
- read-only mandatory reading
- prohibited

A touched surface is editable.

A read-only mandatory-reading surface must be read and obeyed. It must not be edited unless the active bounded pack explicitly moves it into touched scope and states why.

A prohibited surface must neither be edited nor treated as mutable support for convenience changes.

No surface enters touched scope by assumption, adjacency, or convenience.

## State-integrity law

Any test, assertion, or control-surface rule that encodes a temporary or point-in-time state must declare its lifecycle as one of:

- later-state tolerant
- mandatory retire at closeout
- mandatory replace at closeout

A temporary-state surface must not remain in place as if it were permanent law after the state it described has lawfully changed.

## No-retroactive-scope-legalisation law

A file that was not in mutable scope at gate open must not be touched first and legalised later.

If an unlisted file becomes necessary during execution, stop and reopen the bounded pack before touching it.

## Adjacent-governed-surface law

If a gate touches a surface that belongs to a governed block, that governed block must be reconciled before closeout.

A green local slice does not close a gate while another touched governed block remains unreconciled.

## Receipt law

If branch truth changed, update every required receipt surface that records that changed truth.

Required receipt surfaces include:

- the active bounded pack surface when its truth changed
- `config/retained_test_registry.yaml` when test migration status changed
- touched manifest or proof-ownership surfaces when their truth changed
- touched control surfaces whose recorded state changed

A receipt is incorrect if it omits a changed live surface that current live doctrine requires it to record.

## Closeout law

A gate is not closed until all touched live control surfaces and required receipts agree on the same state.

If any touched live control surface or required receipt disagrees, the gate is not closed.

## Live-authority boundary law

The live authority stack is limited to the control documents and active bounded pack surfaces routed by current live doctrine.

Historical support material, retained evidence, and non-authority files do not enter the live authority stack unless a higher-priority live surface explicitly routes them there.

Repo-root `README.md` is not part of the live authority stack.

## Process-closeout rule

Work is not closed if any required process condition remains unmet.

That includes any case where:

- no active bounded pack is routed
- a touched governed surface remains unreconciled
- a required receipt is missing or incorrect
- current repo truth and stated repo truth do not match

A clean narrative does not close a gate.

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

===== END docs/06_PROCESS_AND_TRANCHE_LAW_CORE.md =====

## Local bounded branch: ROADMAP_NEXT_THREE_HELD_GATES_5_4_5_9_5_12_BRANCH_001

Authority opened: the user's instruction `Do the next three gates` opens the next three held rows after `5.8 Parallel Risk Lane` in the held-row matrix: `5.4 Financial and event calendar architecture`, `5.9 Dynamic coefficient and modifier authority`, and `5.12 Opening architecture and placement readiness`.

Scope closed: live online calendar mutation, live market fetching, dependency changes, external network/API clients, concrete broker adapters, broker order submission, live order placement, capital sizing recomputation, serial candidate formation, 5.8 risk generation, unbounded coefficient tuning, hidden strategy changes, source-data ingestion, original Atlas/Bridge JSON, generated Atlas slices, and historical receipts.

Required proof: calendar projection must reject hidden runtime/trading/capital authority keys and emit checked-reference trace; coefficient/modifier replay must be deterministic and identify closed authority classes; opening readiness must consume capital-deployment receipt truth and emit protocol-only handoff material without broker execution; proof must include targeted Tier A tests, named proof blocks, Makefile gates, formatting/lint checks, and cache cleanup.

Completion claim limit: this branch may prove bounded row `5.4`, `5.9`, and `5.12` hardening only. It must not claim online source ingestion, live trading, broker execution, capital deployment policy beyond prior 5.11 contracts, full runtime strategy behaviour, playbook implementation, or exit/close authority.

## Active bounded exception — ROADMAP_PLAYBOOK_LIBRARY_5_10_BRANCH_001

===== BEGIN docs/07_RUNTIME_OWNERSHIP_AND_CONSUMER_LEDGER_CORE.md =====
# 07_RUNTIME_OWNERSHIP_AND_CONSUMER_LEDGER_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Core rule

Each runtime output must have:

- one bounded owner
- one bounded meaning

If two runtime outputs answer different questions, they remain separate.

A later step in the system does not treat two different outputs as interchangeable merely because one is easier to read or easier to carry forward.

## Packet-carriage rule

When a runtime output is carried in a DMPV3 packet, the common outer envelope does not change ownership of the carried output.

A later step in the system still interprets the carried output according to its bounded owner and bounded meaning.

A later step in the system uses the declared packet contract and packet role.

It does not infer ownership or meaning from payload shape alone.

A common outer envelope is not used as a reason to treat:

- runtime preparation output
- financial-calendar reference output
- serial desk-cognition output
- serial stage outputs inside the desk-cognition spine
- the independent parallel risk lane
- the downstream pre-deployment gate
- capital deployment authority
- downstream opening-architecture outputs
- downstream live-inventory architecture outputs
- downstream state-reader outputs
- review-layer outputs

as interchangeable.

## Runtime outputs that must stay separate

The runtime preserves separate outputs for:

- runtime preparation
- financial-calendar reference and projection
- the serial desk-cognition spine
- serial stage outputs inside the desk-cognition spine
- the independent parallel risk lane
- the downstream pre-deployment gate
- capital deployment authority
- downstream opening-architecture outputs
- downstream live-inventory architecture outputs
- downstream state-reader outputs
- review-layer outputs that state what those parts concluded

Those outputs are related in runtime.

They do not collapse into one blended answer.

## Runtime-preparation output

The runtime-preparation path owns the output that admits source truth and prepares runtime-ready truth.

That output answers the question:

- what runtime-ready truth was admitted and prepared for later governed steps

A later step in the system does not treat that output as candidate formation, the independent parallel risk reading, or capital deployment authority.

## Financial-calendar reference output

The financial-calendar reference and projection path owns the output that preserves calendar truth and projected calendar context.

That output answers the question:

- what financial-calendar truth and projected calendar context were preserved for later governed steps

A later step in the system does not treat that output as candidate formation, the independent parallel risk reading, or capital deployment authority.

## Serial desk-cognition output

The serial desk-cognition spine owns the output that forms the candidate.

That output answers the question:

- what candidate did the serial desk-cognition spine form

A later step in the system does not treat that output as if it came from the independent parallel risk lane or from capital deployment authority.

## Serial stage outputs inside the desk-cognition spine

Where serial stage outputs exist inside the desk-cognition spine, including posture-and-risk permission outputs, those outputs remain serial-path outputs.

Those outputs answer bounded stage questions inside the serial desk-cognition path.

They do not answer the same question as:

- the independent parallel risk lane
- capital deployment authority

A later step in the system does not treat a serial stage output as if it were the independent environmental assessment or the fresh-capital decision.

## Independent parallel risk output

The independent parallel risk lane owns the output that assesses the present environment in parallel with the serial desk-cognition spine.

That output answers the question:

- what did the independent parallel risk lane conclude about the present environment

It does not answer the question:

- what candidate was formed

A later step in the system does not treat the independent parallel risk output as candidate formation.

## Downstream pre-deployment output

The downstream pre-deployment gate owns the output that blocks or allows incomplete, disallowed, or unusable candidate material before capital deployment authority acts.

That output answers the question:

- what candidate material was blocked or allowed before fresh-capital deployment judgement

It does not answer the question:

- what candidate was formed
- what the independent parallel risk lane concluded about the environment
- what fresh-capital decision was made against the candidate

A later step in the system does not treat the downstream pre-deployment output as candidate formation, environmental assessment, or capital deployment authority.

## Capital deployment output

Capital deployment authority owns the output that decides whether fresh capital is deployed against the formed candidate, and in what bounded way.

That output answers the question:

- what fresh-capital decision was made against the candidate

It does not answer the question:

- what candidate was formed
- what the independent parallel risk lane concluded about the environment
- what candidate material was blocked or allowed before the fresh-capital decision

A later step in the system does not treat the capital deployment output as candidate formation or as the environmental assessment.

## Downstream opening-architecture outputs

The downstream opening architecture owns shell outputs. Those shell outputs answer these questions and no others:

- what post-serial opening homes exist
- what those homes read from preserved seam truth and repo-native state
- what remains deferred because behavioural policy is not yet installed

Those outputs do not replace:

- serial candidate formation
- the independent parallel risk lane
- the downstream pre-deployment gate
- capital deployment authority

A later step in the system does not treat an opening-architecture receipt as if it were the fresh-capital decision itself.

## Downstream live-inventory architecture outputs

The downstream live-inventory architecture owns shell outputs. Those shell outputs answer these questions and no others:

- what live-position monitoring, management, rotation, exit, and close homes exist
- which repo-native inventory and receipt readers those homes are permitted to consume
- which behavioural rules remain deferred

Those outputs do not answer the question:

- what fresh-capital decision was made against the opening candidate

A later step in the system does not treat live-inventory architecture receipts as if they were opening-authority outputs.

## Downstream repo-native state reader outputs

The downstream state-reader layer owns explicit outputs. Those outputs answer these questions and no others:

- which repo-native capital, position, and receipt surfaces were read
- whether those reads came from execution-record truth or from a truthful placeholder
- where recommendation-history and every-pass receipt concerns are reserved without policy

Those outputs do not answer the question:

- whether to open, add, rotate, exit, or close a position

A later step in the system does not treat a downstream state-read result as if it were a behavioural decision packet.

## Downstream recommendation-history reservation outputs

The downstream bootstrap owns a reservation output. That reservation output answers these questions and no others:

- the existing receipt tables that anchor later same-pass and recent-pass checks
- the future home where recommendation-history reading will live
- that no duplicate-policy or recent-open formula is installed yet

A later step in the system does not treat that reservation as if the duplicate-policy itself already exists.

## Downstream review and receipt outputs

The downstream review layer owns review outputs that state what downstream architecture receipts read, deferred, and preserved.

Those review outputs do not own the operating decisions of those shells.

A later step in the system does not treat a downstream review receipt as if the review layer itself owned opening, management, rotation, exit, or close behaviour.

## Later-step usage law

A later step in the system uses the runtime output that directly answers the question being asked.

A later step in the system does not prefer a less truthful output merely because it is easier to consume.

If a runtime output exists only for compatibility, that output does not replace the clearer live output underneath it.

If a packet reader has both common-envelope metadata and a clearer preserved runtime surface, the clearer preserved runtime surface still owns the answer to the question it was designed to answer.

Compatibility handling is lawful.

Compatibility handling does not erase the more truthful runtime output.

## Review law

The runtime preserves enough separation that the review layer states, in plain language:

- what runtime truth was admitted and prepared
- what financial-calendar truth was preserved
- what candidate was formed
- what serial stage outputs inside the desk-cognition spine concluded
- what the independent parallel risk lane concluded
- what the downstream pre-deployment gate blocked or allowed
- what capital deployment authority decided
- what downstream opening, execution, inventory, and close surfaces preserved

If a reviewer cannot tell which bounded part of the system produced which result, the runtime output model is wrong.

## Ownership conflict law

A runtime output is in conflict if it appears to answer a question outside its bounded role.

That conflict is resolved by one of these actions:

- narrow the output back to its bounded meaning
- preserve a separate output for the different question
- revise the live control documents explicitly and lawfully

The conflict is not hidden by vague wording or blended review output.

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

===== END docs/07_RUNTIME_OWNERSHIP_AND_CONSUMER_LEDGER_CORE.md =====

===== BEGIN docs/07.1_RUNTIME_OWNERSHIP_AND_CONSUMER_LEDGER_WORKING.md =====
# Runtime Ownership and Consumer Ledger — Working Overlay

This working overlay records bounded additions to `docs/07_RUNTIME_OWNERSHIP_AND_CONSUMER_LEDGER_CORE.md`. It does not replace the core ownership law.

## ROADMAP_5_16_PERSISTENCE_LEDGER_RECEIPT_HARDENING_001

Status: active bounded overlay for row `5.16 Persistence, ledgers, and receipts`.

The persistence hardening tranche adds these local ownership rules:

1. A repository that stores durable truth by identifier must reject duplicate identifiers instead of silently overwriting earlier truth.
2. A repository that stores append-only observations or records must preserve insertion order and must not expose keyed overwrite/upsert methods unless a later tranche admits reconciliation policy.
3. All persistence readback and list surfaces must return copies, not mutable internal storage.
4. The receipt ledger owns audit/checkpoint receipts only. It does not own trading decisions, capital/risk judgement, source-data truth, or Atlas admission state.
5. Receipt payloads admitted by the native receipt ledger must be JSON-serialisable so receipts can be written, replayed, diffed, and checked by later proof tooling.
6. Compatibility wrappers may re-export the benchmark-owned persistence home, but wrappers must not become a second writer of durable truth.

The overlay opens no live broker, order, position-sizing, capital-deployment, or risk-weighting behaviour.

## ROADMAP_SERIAL_SPINE_5H_BRANCH_001

Status: active bounded overlay for row `5.7 Serial desk-cognition spine`.

The serial-spine hardening branch adds these local ownership rules:

1. The serial desk-cognition spine owns candidate-formation stage order and stage outputs only.
2. Its six governed stages are:
   - temporal context
   - market regime context
   - options and flow context
   - posture and risk permission
   - playbook eligibility
   - Expression and Execution
3. Serial-stage dependency declarations may refer only to known earlier stages; no stage may depend on a later stage or silently absorb another stage boundary.
4. Serial execution-stage handoff metadata is trace-only and remains before independent-lane handoff, pre-capital-deployment, and outside live-inventory authority.
5. The serial spine must not allocate capital, place orders, size positions, manage live inventory, or become the independent Parallel Risk Lane by implication.
6. Ledger references to the serial desk-cognition spine must use architecture row `5.7`; row `5.13` remains live position and inventory architecture.

The overlay opens no live broker, order, position-sizing, capital-deployment, live-inventory, or Parallel Risk Lane behaviour.

## ROADMAP_EXTERNAL_INGRESS_AUTHORITY_READINESS_5H_BRANCH_001 — 5.1 source-boundary ownership

- Authoritative writer: no durable writer is admitted by this branch. External source material may cross the boundary only as recorded/offline payload material wrapped by typed carriers.
- Primary readers: raw capture, reference/calendar import, prepared ingress, and API boundary tests may read validated carrier outputs.
- Forbidden mixing: carrier validation must not write persistence state, make domain/trading decisions, open credentials, or construct concrete network/API/broker clients.
- Bootstrap/fallback rule: when no live source-interface authority exists, the lawful fallback is checked-in reference material, offline fixtures, recorded API/broker responses, or operator bundles.
- Traceability reference: `ROADMAP_EXTERNAL_INGRESS_AUTHORITY_READINESS_5H_BRANCH_001` and `var/audit/roadmap_external_ingress_authority_5h/ROADMAP_EXTERNAL_INGRESS_AUTHORITY_READINESS_5H_BRANCH_001_RECEIPT.md`.

## ROADMAP_LIVE_POSITION_INVENTORY_5_13_BRANCH_001 — 5.13 live inventory ownership

- Authoritative writer: position and capital-state repositories own durable identifier-keyed state; the live-inventory service reads those repositories and may write only an explicit inventory receipt through the receipt ledger when a receipt id is supplied.
- Primary readers: live inventory, downstream state reader, review/replay, persistence/ledgers, and future exit/close architecture may read the produced snapshot.
- Forbidden mixing: live-inventory snapshots must not perform broker placement, candidate formation, raw-source preparation, capital deployment sizing, or ungoverned capital mutation.
- Exposure rule: exposure summaries may include only exposure values already declared in stored position payloads; the live-inventory service must not invent valuation or risk policy from quantity alone.
- Bootstrap/fallback rule: without repository state, the lawful fallback remains an architecture-only snapshot over explicit `live_ids` with no position payload, capital state, or invented exposure.
- Traceability reference: `ROADMAP_LIVE_POSITION_INVENTORY_5_13_BRANCH_001` and `var/audit/roadmap_live_inventory_5_13/ROADMAP_LIVE_POSITION_INVENTORY_5_13_BRANCH_001_RECEIPT.md`.

## ROADMAP_DOWNSTREAM_CAPITAL_DEPLOYMENT_5_11_BRANCH_001 — capital deployment ownership supplement

- Authoritative writer: `src/nvda_desk/capital/authority.py` owns bounded downstream capital deployment decisions and capital-deployment receipt construction for row `5.11`.
- Primary readers: downstream opening architecture, persistence/receipt ledgers, and review/replay may consume the capital deployment decision and receipt output.
- Lawful inputs: serial candidate material, downstream pre-deployment allow/block truth, parallel-risk packet identity, final-risk receipt identity, capital-state snapshot identity, bounded deployment policy identity, coefficient/modifier bounds. Temporal Context is also required.
- Forbidden mixing: capital deployment authority must not form candidates, generate a Parallel Risk Lane packet, perform serial-stage decisions, open broker placement, mutate live inventory, or treat opening mechanics as part of the capital decision.
- Traceability rule: every deploy/stand-down decision must carry an input trace that names the serial candidate, pre-deployment receipt, final-risk receipt, capital-state snapshot, bounded policy, and any parallel-risk packet consumed.

## Local supplement: bounded 5.8 ownership

- Authoritative writer: `src/nvda_desk/risk/parallel/evaluate.py` owns bounded parallel-risk envelope evaluation and output packet construction for row `5.8`; `src/nvda_desk/services/parallel_risk_lane_service.py` owns service-level coordination into that evaluator.
- Primary readers: downstream capital deployment, final risk/review, persistence/receipt ledgers, and replay may consume the parallel-risk packet and input trace.
- Lawful inputs: admitted Stage Input Bundle temporal/regime/options-flow primitives or the selected serial-runtime temporal stage packet carried through the bootstrap input packet.
- Forbidden mixing: Row 5.8 must not consume or emit capital deployment sizing, broker placement, order execution, candidate formation, coefficient/modifier policy, review explanation authority, or hidden portfolio mutation.
- Traceability rule: every parallel-risk output packet must carry `ParallelRiskInputTrace` naming the bootstrap input packet, bootstrap source, admitted semantic inputs, parent/dependency packets, and any selected serial temporal stage packet.
- Persistence rule: parallel-risk observation persistence is append-only and must reject duplicate `observation_id` values; it remains write-only to downstream callers.

## Local supplement: bounded 5.4 financial/event calendar ownership

- Authoritative writer: `src/nvda_desk/calendar/reference/importer.py` owns checked reference bundle validation; `src/nvda_desk/calendar/reference/projection.py` owns deterministic calendar projection into precursor runtime context.
- Primary readers: prepared ingress, serial desk-cognition, Parallel Risk Lane, and review/replay may consume projected calendar context and its `CalendarAuthorityProjectionTrace`.
- Forbidden mixing: calendar projection must not carry online mutation, live market fetching, candidate formation, trading instruction, broker order, or capital sizing authority.
- Traceability rule: every projected precursor runtime packet must carry checked-reference projection trace naming bundle, artifacts, event IDs, runtime tags, and effective time.

## Local supplement: bounded 5.9 coefficient/modifier ownership

- Authoritative writer: `src/nvda_desk/config/coefficient_authority.py` owns coefficient-authority loading, modifier-packet construction, and deterministic replay receipts for row `5.9`.
- Primary readers: state-conditioned modifiers, serial stage surfaces, Parallel Risk Lane, review/replay, and proof tooling may consume resolved coefficient packets and replay receipts.
- Forbidden mixing: coefficient/modifier replay must not open capital deployment decisions, source-data ingestion, hidden strategy mutation, or unbounded live self-tuning.
- Traceability rule: replay receipts must carry authority id, schema version, owner stage, active modifier ids, resolved coefficient snapshots, closed authority classes, and deterministic fingerprint.

## Local supplement: bounded 5.12 opening/placement ownership

- Authoritative writer: `src/nvda_desk/downstream/opening.py` owns placement readiness evaluation and non-executing placement handoff material for row `5.12`.
- Primary readers: execution bridge, persistence/receipts, live inventory, and review/replay may consume readiness results and protocol-only handoff material.
- Forbidden mixing: opening readiness must not recompute capital sizing, form serial candidates, generate a Parallel Risk Lane packet, implement broker adapters, submit orders, or mutate live inventory.
- Traceability rule: every placement readiness result must identify the consumed capital-deployment receipt id, serial candidate id, placement constraints, and broker boundary mode.

## Local supplement — row 5.10 playbook library ownership

For `ROADMAP_PLAYBOOK_LIBRARY_5_10_BRANCH_001`, playbook-library ownership is bounded as follows:

- authoritative writer: repo-owned playbook registry/config surfaces and playbook contract models;
- primary readers: serial eligibility service, serial eligibility carrier, review/proof surfaces;
- persistence class: configuration/metadata receipt only;
- bootstrap/fallback rule: checked-in example registry may support deterministic tests but is not live trading authority;
- forbidden mixing: no capital deployment, source-ingress authority, hidden coefficient ownership, broker placement, live order execution, or exit/close authority may be written into playbook material;
- target modules: `src/nvda_desk/contracts/playbook.py`, `src/nvda_desk/config/playbook_registry.py`, and `src/nvda_desk/playbooks/library.py`.

Playbook metadata receipts are selection evidence only. They do not authorise capital, orders, broker placement, or exit/close behaviour.

## Local supplement — row 5.14 exit/close ownership

For `ROADMAP_EXIT_CLOSE_5_14_BRANCH_001`, exit/close ownership is bounded as follows:

- authoritative writer: `src/nvda_desk/downstream/exit_close.py` owns bounded exit/close policy decisions, protocol-only close handoff material, and replayable exit/close receipts;
- primary readers: persistence/receipt ledgers, live inventory, review/replay, and future broker-boundary surfaces may consume exit/close receipts;
- persistence class: receipt/replay metadata only;
- bootstrap/fallback rule: deterministic test traces may stand in for admitted downstream receipts, but they are not live trading authority;
- forbidden mixing: exit/close must not place orders, mutate live inventory, recompute capital sizing, form new candidates, ingest external sources, or open concrete broker execution;
- target modules: `src/nvda_desk/contracts/exit_close.py`, `src/nvda_desk/downstream/exit_close.py`, and `src/nvda_desk/services/exit_close_service.py`.

Exit/close receipts are closure-intent evidence only. They do not authorise broker execution, order placement, capital sizing, or live inventory mutation.

===== BEGIN docs/08_TESTING_AND_PROMOTION_CORE.md =====
# 08_TESTING_AND_PROMOTION_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Scope of this document

This document owns:

- environment and install provenance for governed proof
- proof-tier law
- temporary-state proof lifecycle law
- vocabulary constraints inside proof artefacts
- checkpoint and docstring proof requirements where current live doctrine requires them
- documentation and control-surface proof law
- proof receipt law
- promotion and demotion law

## Environment and install provenance law

Governed proof must run in the repo-local declared environment.

That environment must be created from the repo packaging authority and the canonical install flow declared by current live doctrine.

If current live doctrine declares canonical setup commands such as `make install` or equivalent setup commands, those commands are the required environment path unless a higher-priority live surface explicitly routes a different path.

Ad hoc package installation, undeclared environment drift, or missing environment provenance makes governed proof insufficient.

## Proof-tier law

Governed proof is classified into three tiers:

- Tier A: blocking governed proof
- Tier B: bounded shadow proof
- Tier C: historical, observational, or non-blocking proof

A proof artefact is Tier A only when current live doctrine or the active bounded pack explicitly classifies that artefact as Tier A.

A proof artefact does not change tier by habit or convenience.

## Tier A law

Tier A owns the blocking proof required for the current claim.

If a Tier A artefact that owns a touched surface is red, missing, or unreconciled, the work is not ready for promotion.

A green local proof slice is insufficient while another Tier A artefact that owns a touched surface remains unreconciled.

## Tier B law

Tier B is lawful for bounded shadow checking that is relevant to the current tranche but that is not blocking by current live doctrine.

Tier B debt does not block promotion unless live doctrine or the active bounded pack explicitly promotes it to blocking status.

Tier B must not be presented as Tier A.

## Tier C law

Tier C contains historical, observational, predecessor, or cleanup proof that is not blocking for the current claim unless live doctrine or the active bounded pack explicitly promotes it.

Tier C must not remain treated as blocking after live doctrine and router truth move on.

## Existing Tier A fallout declaration law

If a gate changes a surface in a way that changes the live Tier A boundary for an existing block, the active bounded pack must declare the exact pre-existing Tier A proof files or families that are permitted to receive bounded fallout repair.

If no pre-existing Tier A fallout repair is authorised, the active bounded pack must say `none; stop and re-plan` before those files are touched.

## Packet-carriage proof law

If a tranche changes live packet-carriage law, packet-envelope law, or packet readers, Tier A proof must cover the changed packet families.

That proof must include:

- envelope conformance proof
- producer-side emission proof
- consumer-side acceptance proof
- preserved-seam distinction proof where packet carriage crosses bounded ownership surfaces
- invalid or degraded packet proof where the changed packet family lawfully emits those states

A packet-carriage tranche is incomplete if it proves only packet production or only packet consumption.

## Packet numeric-integrity proof law

If a tranche changes packet carriage for numeric fields, Tier A proof must include representative numeric-integrity cases for the changed packet family.

Those cases must cover the forms that matter for the changed contracts, including where applicable:

- signed values
- zero values
- decimal precision
- percentage scale
- magnitude-sensitive values where `0.01` and `0.0001` are not equivalent

A packet-carriage tranche is incomplete if numeric meaning drifts while envelope proof remains green.

## Packet state-semantics proof law

If a tranche changes packet carriage for a field whose state meaning depends on omission, `null`, or an explicit state value, Tier A proof must cover those distinctions for the changed packet family.

A packet-carriage tranche is incomplete if the migrated contract makes those states ambiguous.

## Install record law

Where governed proof is required, the execution record must make the environment path inspectable.

At minimum, the record must show the canonical setup command or commands that were used for the proof run.

If the environment path cannot be shown, the proof claim is incomplete.

## Temporary-state proof law

Any proof artefact that encodes a temporary or point-in-time state must declare its lifecycle as one of:

- later-state tolerant
- mandatory retire at closeout
- mandatory replace at closeout

A temporary-state proof artefact must not remain in place as if it were permanent law after the state it described has lawfully changed.

## Vocabulary-in-proof law

A non-admitted durable term must not enter a proof artefact that itself becomes lasting repo law.

That restriction applies to artefacts including:

- governed test names
- durable fixture names
- manifest labels
- proof-block names
- contract-facing assertions

Proof artefacts must not become a back door for vocabulary drift.

## Checkpoint proof requirement

If current live doctrine requires a checkpoint declaration for a changed proof artefact, that declaration is part of governed proof.

It must be explicit.

If a required checkpoint declaration is missing, the proof-backed claim is incomplete.

## Docstring proof requirement

If current live doctrine requires a docstring applicability declaration for a changed owned surface, that declaration is part of governed proof.

It must be explicit.

If a required docstring declaration is missing, the proof-backed claim is incomplete.

## Documentation and control-surface proof law

Documentation, router, manifest, retained-test-registry, and control-surface changes are not exempt from governed proof.

If current live doctrine requires those changes to be proven through a governed proof artefact, that proof must run and be recorded before the changed repo truth is claimed.

Documentation work is not self-validating merely because it does not change runtime code.

## Proof receipt law

If proof-backed branch truth changed, update every required proof receipt surface that records that changed truth.

Required proof receipt surfaces include:

- touched proof-manifest or proof-block ownership surfaces when their truth changed
- `config/retained_test_registry.yaml` when tier status changed
- touched control-surface receipts affected by the changed truth

A proof-backed claim is incomplete if a required proof receipt is missing or if it records the wrong state.

## Promotion and demotion law

A proof artefact moves from Tier B or Tier C to Tier A only by explicit live doctrine or lawful active-pack authority.

A proof artefact moves from Tier A only by explicit live doctrine or lawful active-pack authority together with truthful manifest and receipt reconciliation.

Silent promotion and silent demotion are prohibited.

## Proof-closeout rule

Proof-backed work is not ready for promotion if any required proof condition remains unmet.

That includes any case where:

- required blocking proof is red, missing, or unreconciled
- the environment provenance is missing or unlawful
- a temporary-state proof artefact has no declared lifecycle
- a required checkpoint declaration is missing
- a required docstring declaration is missing
- a required proof receipt is missing or incorrect

A persuasive narrative does not make insufficient proof sufficient.

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

# 09_DMPV3_PROTOCOL_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Scope of this document

This document owns:

- the canonical DMPV3 outer envelope
- packet-carriage field law
- producer identity law
- packet role and behaviour-class law
- lineage law
- validation and summary carriage law
- bounded DMP extension law
- the rule that all live packetised outputs use the canonical DMPV3 outer shell

## Core rule

The repo uses one canonical DMPV3 outer envelope for live inter-module packet carriage.

That outer envelope normalises carriage.

It does not merge module responsibility, runtime ownership, or downstream meaning.

If a runtime output is packetised, it must use the canonical DMPV3 shell.

A live module does not keep an alternative outer packet grammar.

## Canonical outer envelope

The live DMPV3 outer envelope is the `DmpV3Envelope` surface in `src/nvda_desk/contracts/packets.py`.

A live DMPV3 packet must carry these outer-envelope fields:

- `specversion`
- `dmpversion`
- `id`
- `source`
- `type`
- `time`
- `datacontenttype`
- `dataschema`
- `traceid`
- `runid`
- `scenarioid`
- `packetrole`
- `producer`
- `lineage`
- `validation`
- `summary`
- `data`
- `dmpextensions`

The repo-facing typed packet helper surface is `src/nvda_desk/contracts/packets.py` via `DmpV3Packet` and `DeskModulePacketV3`.

Compatibility wrappers under `src/nvda_desk/schemas/dmp_envelope.py` and `src/nvda_desk/schemas/dmp.py` do not own the live truth.

## Producer identity law

Every live DMPV3 packet must declare a bounded producer identity.

The producer surface must carry:

- `module_id`
- `module_version`
- `module_instance_id`
- `grammar_role`
- `behaviour_class`

`stage_name` must be omitted only when no narrower stage label exists.

Producer identity must describe the emitting module truthfully.

Producer identity must not be backfilled from payload shape alone.

## Packet role and behaviour-class law

Packet interpretation must use the declared outer carriage surfaces.

A packet reader must use:

- `packetrole`
- `producer.grammar_role`
- `producer.behaviour_class`
- `dataschema`

A packet reader must not infer carriage meaning from payload structure alone.

A shared outer envelope does not make two packetised outputs interchangeable.

Runtime ownership remains governed by `docs/07_RUNTIME_OWNERSHIP_AND_CONSUMER_LEDGER_CORE.md`.

## Lineage law

Every live DMPV3 packet must carry bounded lineage.

Where relevant, lineage must state:

- `parentids`
- `dependencyids`
- `derivedfrom`
- `replayref`
- `sourceartifactids`
- `inputfingerprint`
- `reviewtraceid`
- `replaytraceid`
- `decisiontraceid`

If an emitted packet depends on upstream packets, those upstream packet identities must be declared in lineage rather than left implicit.

If a packet is derived from authoritative source artefacts, those artefact identities must be declared in lineage.

## Validation law

Every live DMPV3 packet must carry a validation record.

A packet with no declared violations is `valid`.

A packet with declared carriage violations is `degraded`.

Validation carriage must not be omitted merely because the payload model validated.

## Summary law

Every live DMPV3 packet must carry a human-facing summary.

The summary must include:

- `headline`
- `keyflags`
- `humanstate`

The summary must describe the emitted packet truthfully.

The summary is an aid for inspection.

It does not replace the payload contract.

## Extension law

`dmpextensions` carries bounded compatibility metadata only.

`dmpextensions` must not be used to replace or weaken the canonical outer-envelope fields.

The repo-owned `nvda` extension block is reserved for bounded compatibility metadata including:

- execution context
- schema identifiers
- semantic dependency annotations

A custom extension must merge without erasing the canonical repo-owned `nvda` compatibility leaves.

## Canonical builder law

Live packet builders must emit the canonical shell through the repo-owned builders:

- `src/nvda_desk/contracts/packets.py`
- `src/nvda_desk/packets/dmp_packets.py`
- explicit compatibility wrappers only where needed, such as `src/nvda_desk/schemas/dmp_envelope.py` and `src/nvda_desk/schemas/dmp.py`

A module-specific packet-specialised wrapper is lawful for a module's own dataschema and module identity.

It still emits the canonical DMPV3 shell.

## Emitter coverage law

The canonical DMPV3 shell applies to live packetised outputs including:

- serial stage packets
- imported-module packets
- financial-calendar reference packets
- the independent parallel risk lane packet
- review packet carriage where runtime outputs are packetised

A module that emits a live packetised output does not keep or reintroduce an alternative outer protocol.

## Native Architecture Blueprint boundary

Within native code-structure architecture scope, this document must be read with `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

`docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md` owns target package placement, component form, seams, ownership, persistence, test-family mapping, reservations, module allocation, and traceability for native code-structure architecture.

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

All durable terms used by live control documents must be governed by `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` or lawfully admitted by the vocabulary process before they become canonical language.

===== END docs/09_DMPV3_PROTOCOL_CORE.md =====

===== BEGIN docs/09.1_DMPV3_PROTOCOL_WORKING.md =====
# 09.1_DMPV3_PROTOCOL_WORKING

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.

## Local supplement

One bounded local supplement is admitted below. The core file otherwise governs in full.

## DMPV3 temporal mapping subset

DMPV3 inherits the repo-wide source temporal standard admitted in `docs/04.1_TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE_WORKING.md`.
This section defines only the DMPV3 temporal mapping subset.
It does not restate or replace the full source temporal law.

`packet.time` is packet emission time.
`packet.time` is not source observation time.

Where packet carriage, payload content, lineage, persistence, or replay/proof surfaces need source observation time, that source observation time must be preserved separately through the admitted source temporal fields where relevant.
That preservation uses `source_time_raw`, `source_time_kind`, `source_timezone_raw`, `source_time_utc`, `source_date`, and `time_granularity` rather than overloading `packet.time`.

If the source truth is date-level only, the carried surface must preserve `source_date` and must not invent a finer source timestamp.
If the source truth is lawful local-clock time with a declared timezone or offset, the carried surface must preserve `source_time_raw`, `source_timezone_raw`, and `source_time_utc` consistently.
If a workbook-derived specimen blends fields with different source dates, packeted or packet-adjacent surfaces must preserve field-level provenance rather than fabricate one false same-moment source observation timestamp.

DMPV3 must not invent source-time precision the source did not provide.

## Rules

- Do not weaken or silently reinterpret the core companion.
- Record only bounded mutable execution-local material here.
- If this file has no admitted local material, the core file governs.

## Prepared context and review packet subset

`prepared_context_packet` is the canonical DMPV3 packet role for bounded prepared-runtime-context handoff material. It carries `producer.module_id=prepared_ingress`, `producer.grammar_role=prepared_runtime_context`, `producer.behaviour_class=preparation_output`, and `producer.stage_name=runtime_context_preparation`.

`review_explanation_packet` is the canonical DMPV3 packet role for deterministic review/explanation material. It carries `producer.module_id=review_explanation`, `producer.grammar_role=review_explanation`, and `producer.behaviour_class=review_only_output`.

Both packet roles preserve lineage and proof material. Neither role authorises live trading, capital deployment, risk weighting, order placement, or source-time precision invention.

# 10_VOCABULARY_AND_TERM_GOVERNANCE_CORE

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.


## Frozen rules

- one durable term must map to one governed meaning
- aliases must be explicit rather than accidental
- compatibility labels must not silently become canonical runtime truth
- durable terms must not be admitted into docs, schemas, manifests, receipts, or code unless they are already governed or the same bounded gate lawfully updates the vocabulary system first

## Authoritative artefacts

Use these together:

- `docs/vocabulary/README.md`
- `docs/vocabulary/CONSOLIDATION_WORKFLOW.md`
- `src/nvda_desk/schemas/vocabulary.py`
- `scripts/build_canonical_vocabulary.py`
- `data/vocabulary/canonical_vocabulary.json`

## Admission rule

The vocabulary system is an admission system, not a free-form glossary.

Candidate terms must be classified as exactly one of:
- `enrich_existing`
- `add_new`
- `alias_only`
- `quarantine`

A new durable term must land in live repo surfaces only after lawful admission.

The detailed supporting material remains in:
- `docs/vocabulary/README.md`
- `docs/vocabulary/CONSOLIDATION_WORKFLOW.md`
- `docs/reference/vocabulary_dictionary_system_audit.md`

It does not override the authority hierarchy defined by `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md`.

# 10.1_VOCABULARY_AND_TERM_GOVERNANCE_WORKING

All repo paths in the numbered control stack are repo-root-relative.

## Local supplement

One bounded local supplement is admitted for the currently routed bounded work programme.

Admitted terms for the current bounded pack:

- `donor evidence` [`add_new`] = retained legacy source consulted as historical evidence only, not as live native authority
- `behaviour block` [`add_new`] = the donor-reading subdivision inside a file used for later intent recovery
- `intent unit` [`add_new`] = the atomic rebuild unit; one lawful owner, one bounded behavioural job, and one bounded proof family
- `atomic intent unit` [`alias_only`] = explicit label for one `intent unit` handled one at a time
- `target owner` [`add_new`] = the lawful owner in the rebuilt architecture that receives a recovered intent unit
- `provenance` [`enrich_existing`] = the minimal evidence pointer for a recovered claim: donor file path, donor line range, and evidence type
- `leaf` [`add_new`] = one bounded execution unit inside the active tranche or execution plan
- `authority intake gate` [`add_new`] = the pre-edit control step that declares in-scope authority files, full-read status, gate pass/fail criterion, and authority conflicts before repo claims or file changes
- `execution read sequence` [`enrich_existing`] = the ordered substantive repo read sequence owned by `docs/06_PROCESS_AND_TRANCHE_LAW_CORE.md`
- `active bounded pack` [`enrich_existing`] = the currently routed bounded work pack declared by live route surfaces for one active repo objective
- `active bounded pack surface` [`enrich_existing`] = the specific file that declares or constrains the active bounded pack for the current route

- `state_conditioned_modifier` [`add_new`] = a governed modifier policy that can alter only admitted coefficient surfaces under explicit state conditions
- `bounded_dynamic_coefficient` [`add_new`] = a coefficient whose effective value may vary only inside declared hard bounds and governed promotion authority
- `review_horizon` [`add_new`] = the coefficient review window or trigger used for promotion evidence, not live self-tuning
- `champion_challenger_evaluation` [`add_new`] = the non-deploying comparison pattern where challenger coefficient settings require promotion before live authority
- `live_paper_historical_evaluation_separation` [`add_new`] = the rule that live-paper evidence and historical evidence remain distinct and cannot directly mutate live coefficient authority

===== BEGIN docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md =====
# 11 Native Architecture Blueprint Core

Status: accepted normative architecture authority  
Acceptance receipt: `var/audit/ledgers/native_blueprint/acceptance_receipt.json`  
Document class: primary code-structure architecture document under the `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md` authority hierarchy  
Scope: native codebase structure, sequential architecture surfaces, ownership, seams, persistence, tests, reservations, and migration traceability  
Purpose: prevent messy code from being created, preserved, normalised, or migrated into the native repo

---

## 0. Core law

The native NVDA codebase must be structured as a clean target-state codebase.

All production code, tests, contracts, repositories, adapters, schemas, services, configuration surfaces, and migration work must conform to the target architecture defined by this document.

Current-state mess does not define future structure. Legacy convenience does not define target ownership. Migration pressure does not justify blurred package placement, hidden coupling, mixed responsibilities, or unclear seams.

A code change is not architecturally valid unless the affected capability can be assigned to:

1. a target package or subpackage;
2. a target module or component form;
3. an owning authority;
4. a lawful seam and carrier, where a boundary is crossed;
5. a persistence owner, where state is stored or read;
6. a test family, where behaviour or structure requires proof;
7. a traceability path, where migration or current-state carry-forward is involved;
8. an honest structural status: `Locked`, `Provisional`, or `Reserved`;
9. an honest implementation-truth status: `implemented`, `thin_seam`, `placeholder`, `reserved`, `future_only`, `legacy_only`, or `rejected`.

---

## 1. What this document is

This document is the target architecture benchmark for the native deterministic NVDA repo.

It governs package, module, component, seam, persistence, test, reservation, migration-allocation, traceability, and sequential runtime/control-surface structure.

It defines what good code structure means before implementation, migration planning, or refactor execution occurs.

It is first-class native code-structure authority within the `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md` authority hierarchy. It does not outrank the normative hierarchy that governs it.

It does not need source-provenance language inside the normative body.

---

## 2. What this document is not

This document is not a migration sequence.

This document is not a file-by-file refactor plan.

This document is not a work ledger.

This document is not an implementation task list.

This document is not permission to collapse target architecture into current-state compromises.

This document does not make messy code acceptable because equivalent behaviour already exists somewhere in the repo.

---

## 3. Status models

This document uses two separate status models. They must not be conflated.

### 3.1 Structural status

Every target architecture item uses one of three structural status values.

| Status | Meaning | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `Locked` | The benchmark shape is fixed now. | Code must conform to it. |
| `Provisional` | The likely target shape is known, but final details are not fixed. | Code may proceed only within the stated bounded shape. |
| `Reserved` | The target role is known, but the final allocation is unresolved. | Code must not guess or hard-code the unresolved allocation. |

A structural status value is not a progress label.

`Reserved` is not a defect. Hidden ambiguity is a defect.

### 3.2 Implementation-truth status

Every sequential architecture surface must carry exactly one implementation-truth status.

| Status | Meaning | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `implemented` | Native code and proof surfaces currently satisfy the surface. | The claim must cite native code and proof evidence. |
| `thin_seam` | A lawful native seam exists, but behaviour is incomplete. | The surface must not be described as mature implemented behaviour. |
| `placeholder` | A named native shell exists with little or no behaviour. | The surface must not be treated as behaviourally complete. |
| `reserved` | Architecture reserves the surface, and native implementation is not present. | Later admissibility work is required before implementation. |
| `future_only` | Planned future surface with no current native implementation. | It must not be used as current runtime truth. |
| `legacy_only` | Found only in legacy or Atlas evidence. | It must not become native architecture without later admissibility work. |
| `rejected` | Not part of native architecture. | The reason for rejection must be stated. |

A surface marked `implemented` must cite native code and proof evidence.

A surface marked `thin_seam`, `placeholder`, `reserved`, or `future_only` must not be described as completed runtime behaviour.

Durable architecture terms used by this document must be governed by `docs/10_VOCABULARY_AND_TERM_GOVERNANCE_CORE.md` before freeze. A target term that is not governed, admitted, aliased, or explicitly quarantined by the vocabulary process is not accepted normative language.

---

## 4. Primary control surfaces

The native architecture is controlled through the following surfaces.

| Control surface | Governs | Required decision before code is accepted |
|---|---|---|
| Sequential Native Architecture Surfaces | Top-to-bottom runtime/control architecture from source ingress to review and proof. | Which architecture surface owns the capability. |
| Target Structure | Target package and subpackage families. | Where the code belongs. |
| Component Skeleton | Module-level component shape. | What component form the code takes. |
| Ownership Matrix | Capability ownership and forbidden placement. | Who owns the capability. |
| Seam Contracts | Boundary crossings and carrier rules. | How data crosses a boundary. |
| Bridge and Shell Policy | Transitional, advisory, optional, and shell surfaces. | Whether partial surfaces are lawful and how they are labelled. |
| Persistence Architecture | State ownership, writers, readers, repositories, and stores. | Who may write and who may read durable state. |
| Test Architecture | Required architecture test families. | What must prove the structure or behaviour. |
| Reservations | Bounded unresolved allocations. | What must stay open rather than guessed. |
| Module Allocation Appendix | Migration-facing module allocation detail. | How current or donor material maps into target structure without overriding it. |
| Traceability Appendix | Current-state and donor traceability. | Why carried-forward material exists and where it must land. |

No other control surface may silently override these surfaces.

---

## 5. Sequential Native Architecture Surface law

The native architecture must be described top-to-bottom from source ingress to review and proof.

The blueprint must not start at the serial desk-cognition spine.

The following eighteen architecture surfaces are the required order for native architecture discussion, review, migration allocation, and Bridge satisfaction mapping.

The term `architecture surface` is the normative term in this document. The informal architecture metaphor must not be used as normative language.

### 5.1 External source interfaces and API ingress

| Required field | Normative value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Lawful outer boundary for external market, options, broker, calendar, and reference inputs before native preparation. |
| Target owner | ingress and adapter authorities. |
| Structural status | `Locked`. |
| Implementation-truth status | `thin_seam`. |
| Lawful inputs | external payloads; broker/API responses; checked-in reference artefacts; operator source bundles. |
| Lawful outputs | typed native ingress carriers; adapter outputs; source validation results. |
| Prohibited decisions | domain decisions; trading decisions; persistence authority; hidden mutation of downstream state. |
| Downstream consumers | raw capture; calendar/reference architecture; prepared ingress. |
| Vocabulary terms used | external source interface; API ingress; adapter; typed carrier. |
| Proof interface | adapter tests; carrier validation tests; source-shape proof; boundary rejection proof. |
| Seam boundaries | external data crosses only through explicit adapters and typed carriers. |
| Evidence basis | The `thin_seam` status remains until native code and proof evidence justify a lawful `implemented` status in the acceptance ledger. |

### 5.2 Raw capture and source temporal law

| Required field | Normative value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Raw capture, source timestamp treatment, source session context, and preservation of source-time truth before runtime interpretation. |
| Target owner | raw capture and source-temporal authorities. |
| Structural status | `Locked`. |
| Implementation-truth status | `thin_seam`. |
| Lawful inputs | typed ingress carriers; source timestamps; capture metadata; session/source metadata. |
| Lawful outputs | raw capture records; source-time-normalised carriers; capture receipts. |
| Prohibited decisions | runtime regime decisions; candidate formation; capital decisions; retrospective timestamp rewriting. |
| Downstream consumers | raw signal families; financial/event calendar architecture; prepared ingress. |
| Vocabulary terms used | raw capture; source temporal law; capture receipt; source timestamp. |
| Proof interface | capture fixture tests; timestamp invariant tests; replay determinism tests. |
| Seam boundaries | raw capture may feed preparation and audit ledgers but must not bypass preparation into cognition or downstream authority. |
| Evidence basis | The `thin_seam` status remains until native code and proof evidence justify a lawful `implemented` status in the acceptance ledger. |

### 5.3 Raw signal families

| Required field | Normative value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Native grouping of raw and minimally-derived signal families before runtime preparation. |
| Target owner | raw signal family authorities. |
| Structural status | `Locked`. |
| Implementation-truth status | `thin_seam`. |
| Lawful inputs | raw market data; raw options chain data; raw volatility references; raw calendar/event context; source-normalised capture outputs. |
| Lawful outputs | typed raw signal family carriers and eligibility for preparation. |
| Prohibited decisions | runtime scoring; play selection; posture/risk decisions; downstream deployment decisions. |
| Downstream consumers | prepared ingress; temporal/context preparation; calendar/event context. |
| Vocabulary terms used | raw signal family; market snapshot; options surface; volatility reference. |
| Proof interface | signal schema tests; fixture round-trip tests; source-family boundary tests. |
| Seam boundaries | raw signal families stay upstream of cognition. |
| Evidence basis | The `thin_seam` status remains until native code and proof evidence justify a lawful `implemented` status in the acceptance ledger. |

### 5.4 Financial and event calendar architecture

| Required field | Normative value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Checked calendar truth, event reference material, event projection, and calendar-derived runtime authority. |
| Target owner | financial calendar authority. |
| Structural status | `Locked`. |
| Implementation-truth status | `thin_seam`. |
| Lawful inputs | checked-in calendar/reference sources; event records; market-session metadata; source-time references. |
| Lawful outputs | calendar authority packets; event-context projections; session/event context for preparation. |
| Prohibited decisions | candidate formation; trading instruction; capital sizing; hidden online calendar mutation. |
| Downstream consumers | prepared ingress; temporal context; serial desk-cognition spine; Parallel Risk Lane. |
| Vocabulary terms used | Financial Calendar Crosswalk; event calendar; calendar authority; event projection. |
| Proof interface | calendar import tests; projection tests; checked-reference parity tests; event-window tests. |
| Seam boundaries | calendar authority enters runtime through governed projection carriers. |
| Evidence basis | The `thin_seam` status remains until native code and proof evidence justify a lawful `implemented` status in the acceptance ledger. |

### 5.5 Prepared ingress and runtime context preparation

| Required field | Normative value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Conversion of raw/captured/reference material into runtime-ready typed inputs. |
| Target owner | runtime preparation authority. |
| Structural status | `Locked`. |
| Implementation-truth status | `thin_seam`. |
| Lawful inputs | raw signal family carriers; calendar authority packets; source-temporal material; configuration authority. |
| Lawful outputs | prepared runtime substrate; stage input carriers; context packets. |
| Prohibited decisions | serial stage decisions; risk decisions; capital decisions; direct persistence writes unless explicitly assigned. |
| Downstream consumers | DMPV3 packet carriage; serial desk-cognition spine; parallel risk lane. |
| Vocabulary terms used | runtime preparation; prepared substrate; context preparation; stage input carrier. |
| Proof interface | preparation tests; contract tests; replay fixture tests; rejection tests for incomplete inputs. |
| Seam boundaries | preparation is the lawful seam between upstream evidence and runtime cognition. |
| Evidence basis | The `thin_seam` status remains until native code and proof evidence justify a lawful `implemented` status in the acceptance ledger. |

### 5.6 DMPV3 packet carriage

| Required field | Normative value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | True inter-module packet carriage at deliberate runtime boundaries. |
| Target owner | DMPV3 packet authority. |
| Structural status | `Locked`. |
| Implementation-truth status | `thin_seam`. |
| Lawful inputs | serial runtime outputs; admitted packet payloads; bounded parallel-risk inputs; runtime receipts. |
| Lawful outputs | DMPV3 envelopes; DeskModulePacketV3 carriers; packet receipts. |
| Prohibited decisions | raw ingress carriage; every internal serial step; business logic; persistence truth by implication. |
| Downstream consumers | parallel risk lane; downstream capital deployment authority; persistence/receipts; review/replay. |
| Vocabulary terms used | DMPV3; packet carriage; envelope; DeskModulePacketV3. |
| Proof interface | packet schema tests; boundary fixture tests; replay equivalence tests; no-DMP-upstream checks. |
| Seam boundaries | DMPV3 begins at deliberate inter-module packet boundaries. |
| Evidence basis | The `thin_seam` status remains until native code and proof evidence justify a lawful `implemented` status in the acceptance ledger. |

### 5.7 Serial desk-cognition spine

| Required field | Normative value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Ordered candidate formation through the serial runtime stages. |
| Target owner | serial desk-cognition authority. |
| Structural status | `Locked`. |
| Implementation-truth status | `thin_seam`. |
| Lawful inputs | prepared runtime substrate; temporal context; Market Regime Context; options/flow context; posture inputs; eligibility inputs; execution-stage inputs. |
| Lawful outputs | serial candidate outputs; stage packets; execution candidate material. |
| Prohibited decisions | parallel risk independence; capital deployment authority; live position management; persistence writes unless explicitly assigned. |
| Downstream consumers | DMPV3 packet carriage; parallel risk lane; downstream pre-deployment gate; capital deployment authority; review. |
| Vocabulary terms used | serial desk-cognition spine; temporal stage; regime stage; options/flow stage; posture; eligibility; execution. |
| Proof interface | stage-order tests; stage contract tests; deterministic replay tests; no-capital-leakage tests. |
| Seam boundaries | serial cognition emits bounded outputs and must not decide capital deployment. |
| Evidence basis | The `thin_seam` status remains until native code and proof evidence justify a lawful `implemented` status in the acceptance ledger. |

### 5.8 Parallel risk lane

---

## 6. Target Structure law

Target Structure governs package and subpackage placement.

Each target package or subpackage must have:

1. one bounded responsibility;
2. allowed contents;
3. forbidden contents;
4. preferred component forms;
5. an honest structural status.

Code must be placed by target responsibility, not by historical file location, local convenience, or similarity of names.

A package must not become a dumping ground for unrelated services, schemas, adapters, repositories, orchestration, tests, and configuration.

A subpackage must not own behaviour that belongs to another layer simply because the dependency is nearby.

If a capability does not have a lawful package home, the capability is `Reserved` until the target home is resolved.

---

## 7. Component Skeleton law

Component Skeleton governs the internal shape of modules.

Every module must have a coherent primary responsibility.

A component must use the correct form for its job:

| Component form | Owns | Must not own |
|---|---|---|
| Adapter | External boundary translation. | Core domain decisions. |
| Contract | Data shape crossing a boundary. | Runtime behaviour. |
| Schema or model | Validated data structure. | Service orchestration. |
| Service | Runtime operation or domain action. | Persistence authority unless explicitly assigned. |
| Repository or store | Durable state read/write boundary. | Candidate formation or strategy logic. |
| Orchestrator | Ordered coordination. | Hidden business rules outside authorised stages. |
| Configuration authority | Governed parameters and allowed values. | Ad hoc runtime mutation. |
| Test surface | Proof of structure or behaviour. | Production decision logic. |

A module must not mix component forms unless the target architecture explicitly allows that mixture.

Helper functions do not define ownership. Ownership is defined by bounded responsibility and target component form.

---

## 8. Ownership Matrix law

Ownership Matrix governs capability ownership.

Every major capability or concept must have:

1. a target owner;
2. a target module or area;
3. a preferred form;
4. an input carrier, where applicable;
5. an output carrier, where applicable;
6. forbidden placement;
7. an honest status.

A capability must not be implemented in two places with competing authority.

A capability must not be placed in an adapter, schema, repository, or test merely because that location is convenient.

A capability must not be carried forward from legacy code until its target owner is known or explicitly reserved.

Where ownership is unresolved, the correct action is `Reserved`, not implicit implementation.

---

## 9. Seam Contract law

Seam Contracts govern boundaries between architecture surfaces.

A seam must define:

1. upstream owner;
2. downstream owner;
3. carrier type;
4. required invariants;
5. persistence side effects;
6. allowed variants;
7. forbidden variants;
8. carrier family;
9. seam category;
10. traceability reference, where migration is involved.

A boundary crossing must use an explicit carrier or contract.

Data must not cross architecture boundaries through hidden imports, shared mutable objects, implicit globals, undocumented dictionaries, or test-only shortcuts.

A seam must not become an owner of the decisions it transports.

A carrier must not become business logic.

A downstream surface must not read upstream internals if the seam defines a lawful carrier.

---

## 10. Bridge and Shell Policy law

Bridge and Shell Policy governs transitional, advisory, optional, and shell surfaces.

A partial surface is lawful only when it is explicitly labelled and bounded.

A bridge or shell must define:

1. target home;
2. allowed behaviour;
3. required label;
4. forbidden behaviour;
5. target resolution path;
6. honest status.

A shell must not masquerade as completed behaviour.

A bridge must not become permanent architecture by inertia.

An advisory surface must not make binding runtime decisions unless the target architecture assigns that authority.

A transitional surface must have a resolution path or a reservation.

---

## 11. Persistence Architecture law

Persistence Architecture governs durable state.

Every persistence domain must define:

1. authoritative writer;
2. primary readers;
3. persistence class;
4. bootstrap or fallback rule;
5. forbidden mixing;
6. target repository or store module;
7. traceability reference, where migration is involved.

Only the authoritative writer may write durable truth for a persistence domain.

Readers must consume through lawful repository, store, contract, or query surfaces.

Persistence must not be mixed with candidate formation, orchestration, adapter translation, schema validation, or tests unless explicitly assigned.

State must not be duplicated into competing durable stores without a governed reconciliation rule.

A bootstrap or fallback path must not silently become normal runtime authority.

---

## 12. Test Architecture law

Test Architecture governs proof families, not individual test-case trivia.

Every benchmark family must map to a required test family.

A test family must define:

1. primary scope;
2. target subsystem;
3. failure signal;
4. notes or constraints;
5. honest status.

Tests must prove the architecture claim they are attached to.

A green test does not make code architecturally valid if the code is in the wrong package, wrong module form, wrong ownership boundary, wrong seam, or wrong persistence domain.

A test must not encode a messy current-state compromise as future architecture truth.

Where code structure is governed, tests must verify the boundary that matters, not merely execute the path.

---

## 13. Reservations and Provisional Allocations law

Reservations govern bounded open questions.

A reservation must define:

1. the open allocation point;
2. why it is not locked yet;
3. bounded options;
4. evidence required to resolve it;
5. current benchmark constraint;
6. owner to decide later;
7. likely target home, where known;
8. traceability reference, where migration is involved.

Unresolved architecture must be visible.

A reserved item must not be implemented as though it were locked.

A provisional item must not expand beyond its bounded options.

A developer must not create a new target home to avoid resolving a reservation.

---

## 14. Module Allocation Appendix law

Module allocation is migration-facing detail.

It assists current-state and donor material in finding target homes.

It must not override Target Structure, Component Skeleton, Ownership Matrix, Seam Contracts, Persistence Architecture, Test Architecture, or Reservations.

A module allocation entry must define:

1. target package;
2. target subpackage;
3. target module;
4. bounded responsibility;
5. preferred form;
6. primary inputs;
7. primary outputs;
8. forbidden co-location;
9. observed current basis, where relevant.

A current file must not be moved or rewritten directly into the native repo unless its target allocation is lawful.

Observed current basis explains what exists. It does not authorise preserving messy structure.

---

## 15. Traceability Appendix law

Traceability is migration evidence.

It explains why current-state or donor material was carried forward.

It must not pollute canonical target architecture.

A traceability entry must define:

1. traceability reference;
2. target surface;
3. donor or current-state source;
4. observed current basis;
5. reason carried forward.

Traceability must not become implementation authority.

A traceability reference is valid only when it leads to a lawful target package, component form, owner, seam, persistence owner, and test family.

---

## 16. Anti-mess law

The following patterns are prohibited:

1. mixed-responsibility modules;
2. business logic inside adapters;
3. orchestration hidden inside schemas or contracts;
4. persistence writes from non-authoritative surfaces;
5. shared mutable state across architecture boundaries;
6. implicit carriers where a seam requires a contract;
7. duplicate owners for the same capability;
8. test fixtures that become runtime authority;
9. migration code that bypasses target ownership;
10. reserved decisions implemented as if locked;
11. provisional decisions expanded beyond their bounded shape;
12. bridge or shell surfaces presented as completed behaviour;
13. code placement based on legacy file location rather than target architecture;
14. current-state compromise treated as native design.

A patch containing any prohibited pattern fails architecture review.

---

## 17. Acceptance rule for code changes

A code change is architecturally acceptable only when the reviewer can answer the following questions without guessing.

| Question | Required answer |
|---|---|
| Where does this belong? | Target package, subpackage, and module. |
| What form is it? | Adapter, contract, schema, service, repository, orchestrator, configuration authority, or test surface. |
| Who owns it? | One target owner. |
| What may it consume? | Lawful inputs or carriers. |
| What may it emit? | Lawful outputs or carriers. |
| What boundary does it cross? | Explicit seam contract, or no boundary crossing. |
| Does it persist state? | Authoritative writer and primary readers, or no persistence. |
| What proves it? | Required test family and failure signal. |
| Is anything unresolved? | `Reserved` or `Provisional` with bounded options. |
| Is any current-state mess being preserved? | No. |

If any answer is missing, the change is incomplete.

If any answer depends on undocumented inference, the change is incomplete.

If any answer contradicts a locked target structure, the change is rejected.

---

## 18. Acceptance rule for this document

This document is sufficient when each in-scope major capability can be assigned to:

1. target package or subpackage;
2. target module;
3. component form;
4. seam owner;
5. persistence owner;
6. test family;
7. traceability path;
8. `Locked`, `Provisional`, or `Reserved` status.

This document fails if it becomes a migration plan, a receipt ledger, a trading-flow essay, or a justification of current-state mess.

---

## 19. One-line rule

Write and refactor code into the clean target architecture; do not create, preserve, normalise, or migrate messy code.

===== END docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md =====
