# tests/AGENTS.override.md

## Purpose

This file governs any task that reads, edits, retires, restores, or creates files under `tests/`.

Greenness is not the target. Truthful protection of real invariants is the target.

This file is subordinate to the current user instruction, repo-root `AGENTS.md`, the active approved `EXEC_PLAN.md`, `PLANS.md`, and the repo normative documents governing the selected subject area, including `docs/08_TESTING_AND_PROMOTION_CORE.md` for test, proof, promotion, demotion, receipt, checkpoint, and docstring-proof law. It provides subtree-specific constraints for work under `tests/`.

## Required read sequence for test work

Before touching a file under `tests/`, read in this order:

1. repo-root `AGENTS.md`
2. `docs/00_CONTROL_SURFACE_ROUTING_INDEX.md`
3. `docs/01_NORMATIVE_CORE.md` and any routed `*.1_*_WORKING.md` companion relevant to the question
4. `docs/10_VOCABULARY_AND_TERM_GOVERNANCE_CORE.md` and `docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md` when durable terms, fixture names, proof labels, or registry terms are touched
5. `docs/08_TESTING_AND_PROMOTION_CORE.md` and `docs/08.1_TESTING_AND_PROMOTION_WORKING.md`
6. `config/retained_test_registry.yaml`
7. `config/proof_blocks.yaml`
8. this file
9. only then the specific native test family, legacy test source, runtime code, fixtures, and retained artefacts in scope

## Authority hierarchy for tests

For test work, the governing hierarchy is:

1. official source material when the task depends on external language, API, package, platform, or tool behaviour
2. repo-root `AGENTS.md` and the numbered normative/control stack
3. vocabulary governance for durable terms
4. `docs/08_TESTING_AND_PROMOTION_CORE.md` for tier, proof, promotion, demotion, receipt, checkpoint, and docstring obligations
5. `config/retained_test_registry.yaml` and `config/proof_blocks.yaml`
6. admitted tests and governed proof artefacts
7. runtime code as the observed implementation surface
8. docstrings as owned explanatory surfaces

Runtime code is never permitted to override normative doctrine, vocabulary governance, admitted proof, or retained-test registry authority.

## Admission and registry rule

No native pytest file is admitted by folder placement alone.

A file under `tests/tier_a`, `tests/tier_b`, or `tests/tier_c` is governed only through explicit registry, proof-block, or active-pack authority.

New tests, promoted tests, demoted tests, archived tests, and migrated tests must update the required receipt surfaces before promotion or completion is claimed.

## Invariant-first rule

Before changing a failing, migrating, or retained test, identify:

1. the invariant the test is intended to protect
2. whether that invariant is still live in the clean rebuild
3. whether the truthful action is re-author, defer, archive, demote, promote, or block

## Source-truth rule

Legacy tests under `legacy_import/tests/` are source evidence only.

A legacy test file is not native authority.

## Code-and-docstring reread rule

Before re-authoring, admitting, promoting, demoting, or materially relying on a native test family:

1. reread the runtime code under test
2. reread the docstrings on the owned runtime surface
3. compare the docstrings to the runtime code
4. compare the test assertions to the governing invariant and runtime code
5. record any material divergence in the relevant receipt surface

If a docstring and runtime code diverge, runtime code is the observed implementation surface only.

That observation does not make runtime code authoritative over normative doctrine, vocabulary governance, admitted tests, or retained-test registry authority.

A proof claim is incomplete until the divergence is declared and the governed follow-up is recorded.

# AGENTS.override.md

## Purpose

This file is the active bounded pack surface for the intent-migration programme.

It governs bounded work that converts legacy donor evidence into native rebuilt behaviour under the current architecture.

The legacy tree is donor evidence only.

The rebuilt repo is the destination.

## Freeze status

This file is mutable only by a bounded governance change that updates the active route and the routed plan surfaces in the same change.

Do not rewrite this file during routine donor reading, intent extraction, mapping, rebuild, or proof work.

Read these files in this exact order:

1. `docs/00_CONTROL_SURFACE_ROUTING_INDEX.md`

## Execution rule

Treat files as inventory and reading units, not as migration units.

Do not mark an intent unit complete until provenance, target owner, and Tier A, Tier B, and Tier C proof obligations are satisfied or truthfully declared out of scope by governing law.

## Touch rule

Do not touch runtime code, tests, doctrine files, donor files, or receipts outside the currently admitted bounded scope.

If a new surface becomes necessary, reopen the bounded pack before touching it.

## Definition of done

Do not claim complete, aligned, reviewed, or ready without direct file evidence.

Do not let the routed plan surfaces go stale when branch truth changes.

authority_id: retained_test_registry_v3
status: active retained-test authority
purpose: This file is the native retained-test authority for Tier A admission during the clean execution rebuild.
rules:
  single_authority_rule: config/retained_test_registry.yaml is the retained-test authority for native retained-test admission, together with live proof-block and active-pack receipt authority where those surfaces lawfully modify test status.
  no_silent_admission_rule: A native pytest file must not enter tests/tier_a, tests/tier_b, or tests/tier_c as governed proof unless this file, a proof block, or a lawful active-pack receipt admits that family and status explicitly.
  hierarchy_rule: Test authority is subordinate to repo-root AGENTS.md, the numbered normative/control stack, vocabulary governance, and docs/08 testing and promotion law.
  vocabulary_rule: Durable terms used in test names, fixture names, proof labels, manifests, registry entries, or contract-facing assertions must be governed by docs/vocabulary/vocabulary_registry.md or lawfully admitted in the same bounded gate.
  code_then_docstring_rule: For every retained or materially relied-upon test family, reread runtime code, reread docstrings, compare docstrings to runtime code, and compare native test assertions to the governing invariant and runtime code before trusting the family.
  docstring_divergence_rule: If a docstring and runtime code diverge, runtime code is the observed implementation surface only; it does not override normative doctrine, vocabulary governance, admitted tests, or this retained-test authority, and the divergence must be declared in the relevant receipt surface.

## 3. What this file is

This file is a whole-repo architecture ledger. It maps the current NVDA10 repo to the eighteen sequential native architecture surfaces defined in `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`.

For each row it records:

- the blueprint row ID and surface name;
- the blueprint owner and status values;
- observed native repo evidence paths;
- observed proof/test surfaces;
- the current blocker or gap;
- the next lawful action.

It is used by the PM and future worker briefs to prevent Atlas/donor candidates from being routed into vague or convenient code locations.

## 4. What this file is not

This file is not:

- an implementation plan;
- a proof result;
- an Atlas-readiness claim;
- permission to edit source, tests, vocabulary, dependencies, or generated Atlas material;
- a replacement for `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md`;
- a second `PLANS.md`.

If this map conflicts with user instruction, `AGENTS.md`, `PLANS.md`, or governing normative docs, the higher authority wins and this map must be marked stale or rebuilt.

## 6. Status rules used

The blueprint requires each target capability to map to a target package, module form, owner, seam, persistence owner where applicable, test family, traceability path, and honest structural and implementation-truth statuses. It defines implementation-truth values as `implemented`, `thin_seam`, `placeholder`, `reserved`, `future_only`, `legacy_only`, or `rejected` in `docs/11_NATIVE_ARCHITECTURE_BLUEPRINT_CORE.md:81-99`.

This replacement is conservative: no row is upgraded to `implemented` merely because code or tests exist. A row remains at its blueprint implementation-truth status unless an acceptance ledger or bounded proof evidence justifies a narrower claim. Where a proof report exists, this map records it as bounded evidence only.

## 7. Whole-repo architecture map

| Row ID | Architecture surface | Target owner | Structural status | Blueprint implementation-truth status | Observed native repo evidence | Observed proof/test evidence | Current blocker or gap | Next lawful action |
